Based on my thorough analysis of the Minecraft-inspired voxel game codebase, I've identified several bugs and potential issues. Let me compile my findings:

## Bug Report: Core Engine Issues

### 1. **Critical: Player Physics - Fall Damage Calculation Using Uninitialized Variable**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/playerMotion.ts`, lines 97-100 and 125-128
- **Issue:** The variable `prevX` and `prevZ` are assigned the same value as `startX` and `startZ` on lines 97-100, making them completely redundant. However, more critically, lines 99-100 reassign them AFTER they're already used. The actual issue is that `vyBeforeMove` is captured at line 96 BEFORE the player's y-velocity is applied, but it should represent the velocity state at the START of physics before any integration. The variable is correct but the logic flow after depenetration (lines 108-113) could cause the `vyBeforeMove` value to be stale if depenetration occurs.
- **Root Cause:** Fall damage is calculated using `vyBeforeMove < -14` at line 125, but depenetration nudges the player up by 1.0 block total (5 iterations × 0.2), which could affect whether this threshold is crossed.
- **Severity:** Medium | **Confidence:** Medium
- **Fix Direction:** Capture `vyBeforeMove` after depenetration, or redesign depenetration to not interfere with fall damage tracking.

### 2. **Logic Bug: Arrow Bounds Check Asymmetry**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/projectileAI.ts`, lines 142-149
- **Issue:** The bounds check uses `< 1` and `> world.sizeX - 1` for X/Z, but `< 0` and `> world.sizeY` for Y. This is inconsistent. If an arrow reaches exactly `world.sizeY` on the Y axis, it won't be removed (the condition is `p.position.y > world.sizeY`, so `===` passes through). The X/Z check uses `< 1` which means arrows at exactly position 0.5 will persist one more frame.
- **Root Cause:** Inconsistent boundary conditions between axes.
- **Severity:** Low | **Confidence:** High
- **Fix Direction:** Standardize to `< 0 || >= world.sizeX` (and Y) or clarify the intended boundary layer.

### 3. **State Bug: Creeper Logic - Missing Post-Detonation State Reset**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.ts`, lines 243-257
- **Issue:** When a creeper detonates at line 250 (`explode(...)`), its `hp` is set to 0 at line 251. However, the mob will then be swept in the post-loop cleanup at line 270. But there's no guarantee the creeper's `fuseTimer` field is explicitly cleared or that it can't re-trigger logic if an exception or unexpected state occurs. More critically: if a creeper is killed by another mechanism (not its own fuse) while `fuseTimer > 0`, the detonation at line 250 can still fire in the same tick, resulting in a dead creeper exploding.
- **Root Cause:** Missing guard: the detonation should check `mob.hp > 0` before detonating, or the fuse should cancel when the creeper is killed externally.
- **Severity:** Medium | **Confidence:** High
- **Fix Direction:** Add `if (mob.hp > 0)` guard before `explode()` at line 250, or reset `fuseTimer` in `removeMobAt`.

### 4. **Off-by-One Error: Spear Stuck Timer Logic**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/spears.ts`, lines 62-66
- **Issue:** When a spear hits terrain and sticks, `stuckTimer` is set to `0` at line 103. Then on the next frame (line 62-66), the check `spear.stuckTimer += dt` and `if (spear.stuckTimer < SPEAR_STUCK_SECONDS)` means a spear will stay stuck for UP TO `SPEAR_STUCK_SECONDS` plus one full frame. This is very minor but creates asymmetry: a spear despawning due to age vs. due to being stuck won't be consistent (the stuck check runs BEFORE the age check at line 69).
- **Root Cause:** Stuck timer increments before the threshold comparison, and it starts at 0, so the first frame after sticking counts as 0 + dt.
- **Severity:** Low | **Confidence:** Medium
- **Fix Direction:** Initialize `stuckTimer` to `dt` or reverse the order of checks.

### 5. **Data Integrity: Inventory Durability Edge Case**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts`, lines 83-97
- **Issue:** In `consumeToolDurability`, if `slot.durability` is undefined, it defaults to `slot.maxDurability` at line 90. However, when the slot is first created via `createSlot`, does it initialize `durability` to `maxDurability`? If not, the first durability loss would read an undefined value. Let me verify: checking the code path, this appears safe because `createSlot` in `items.ts` should set `durability` to `maxDurability`. However, if a saved slot lacks a durability field (corrupted save or old format), this could cause issues. The real bug is: if `durability` is 1 and `amount` is 1, line 91 evaluates `nextDurability = 1 - 1 = 0`, and line 91's condition `<= 0` triggers correctly, clearing the slot. But if `maxDurability` is 0 (impossible, but the code doesn't guard against it), this would create an empty slot immediately.
- **Root Cause:** No validation that `maxDurability > 0`.
- **Severity:** Low | **Confidence:** Medium
- **Fix Direction:** Add guard `if (!slot.maxDurability || slot.maxDurability <= 0) return null;` at the start.

### 6. **Game Logic Bug: Fishing Bobber Water Detection**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/fishing.ts`, lines 78-89
- **Issue:** At line 79, the code computes `wy = Math.floor(fishing.position.y) - 1`. This assumes the bobber is positioned AT the water surface (y + 1 based on line 62). So if the bobber is at y=10.0 (positioned at water surface cell 9), then `wy = floor(10.0) - 1 = 9`, which is correct. However, if the bobber's y position is between integer values (say 9.5), then `floor(9.5) - 1 = 8`, checking the wrong cell. The bobber is spawned at `cell.y + 1` (line 62), where `cell` comes from `waterSurfaceRaycast`. If `cell.y` is a water block, the bobber should be at `cell.y + 1.0` exactly. But floating-point precision could cause issues if the bobber drifts slightly.
- **Root Cause:** Assumes the bobber's Y is an exact integer; any drift breaks validation.
- **Severity:** Low | **Confidence:** Medium
- **Fix Direction:** Compare against `fishing.position.y - 0.5` to be more robust, or round the bobber's Y to an integer on spawn.

### 7. **Potential NaN Bug: Mob Turn Direction**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/mobAI.ts`, line 163
- **Issue:** At line 163, `mob.direction.applyAxisAngle(UP, (deps.rng() - 0.5) * Math.PI).normalize()`. If the direction vector is ever (0, 0, 0) before normalize, the normalize call will produce NaN (0 length vector normalized = NaN/NaN). On line 40 in `spawnDirector.ts`, the direction is initialized as a normalized random vector, so it should never be zero initially. But if a mob is stuck and its direction is zeroed, or if parallel code paths set it to zero, this could fail.
- **Root Cause:** Missing guard on normalize when vector length is 0.
- **Severity:** Low | **Confidence:** Low
- **Fix Direction:** Add `if (mob.direction.lengthSq() > 1e-6)` before the applyAxisAngle call.

### 8. **Spawn Director Index Calculation Bug (High Confidence)**
- **File & Line:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/systems/spawnDirector.ts`, lines 151-156 and 152-162
- **Issue:** In `tickSpawnerDirector`, the spawner index (from the set) is decomposed back into (sx, sy, sz) using:
  ```
  const sy = Math.floor(idx / layer);
  const rem = idx - sy * layer;
  const sz = Math.floor(rem / world.sizeX);
  const sx = rem - sz * world.sizeX;
  ```
  This is the inverse of `index(x, y, z) = x + z * sizeX + y * sizeX * sizeZ`. However, the calculation at line 162 uses `mob.position.z - (sz + 0.5)` when computing the spawner's local cluster cap. But the actual spawner location should be checked: line 158 uses `Math.hypot(player.position.x - (sx + 0.5), player.position.y - (sy + 0.5), player.position.z - (sz + 0.5))` — mixing Y (which is spawner Y coordinate) with player Y (which is head height ~1.6). This is comparing vertical distance incorrectly. The nearby mob count at line 162 uses horizontal distance only (`mob.position.x/z` vs spawner center), which is correct for clustering but line 158 incorrectly includes the Y component in the activation radius check.
- **Root Cause:** Inconsistent distance metric between activation check and clustering check.
- **Severity:** Medium | **Confidence:** High
- **Fix Direction:** Line 158 should use horizontal distance only, not including Y: `Math.hypot(player.position.x - (sx + 0.5), player.position.z - (sz + 0.5))`.

Perfect, confirmed. The glassMaterial reuses the worldMaterial's map texture. Both materials will dispose this texture when their dispose() method is called.

Now let me finalize my comprehensive findings document:

## FINAL BUG REPORT FOR MONECRAFT

I've completed a thorough analysis of the Minecraft-inspired voxel game codebase, focusing on renderer, input, audio, and React integration. Here are the real correctness bugs I found:

### BUG #1: Double-Disposal of Shared Block Atlas Texture (HIGH CONFIDENCE, MEDIUM SEVERITY)

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`, lines 130-150 and 549-550

**Issue:** 
- Line 131: `worldMaterial` is created with `map: createBlockAtlasTexture()`
- Line 138: `glassMaterial` is created with `map: this.worldMaterial.map` (same texture reference)
- In `dispose()` (lines 549-550), both materials call `.dispose()` which also disposes their texture maps
- This causes the same texture to be disposed twice, potentially causing WebGL errors or memory leaks

**Code Snippet:**
```typescript
this.worldMaterial = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
  map: createBlockAtlasTexture(),  // line 131
  ...
});
this.glassMaterial = new THREE.MeshStandardMaterial({
  map: this.worldMaterial.map,     // line 138 - SAME TEXTURE
  ...
});
// In dispose():
this.worldMaterial.dispose();       // disposes the texture
this.glassMaterial.dispose();       // tries to dispose the SAME texture again
```

**Suggested Fix:** 
Store the texture in a field, dispose it explicitly once after both materials are disposed, or only call dispose() on one material and let Three.js handle the other.

---

### BUG #2: Double-Disposal of Sky Textures (HIGH CONFIDENCE, MEDIUM SEVERITY)

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/skyView.ts`, lines 92-106 and 141-155

**Issue:**
- SpriteMaterials are created with texture maps (sunMat with sunTex, moonMat with moonTex, cloudMat with cloudTex)
- In `dispose()`, both the materials AND their textures are disposed separately
- Material.dispose() already disposes the texture, causing double-disposal

**Code Snippet:**
```typescript
const sunMat = new THREE.SpriteMaterial({ map: sunTex, ... });  // line 94
const moonMat = new THREE.SpriteMaterial({ map: moonTex, ... }); // line 95
const cloudMat = new THREE.MeshBasicMaterial({ map: cloudTex, ... }); // line 106

// In dispose():
sunMat.dispose();    // disposes sunTex
moonMat.dispose();   // disposes moonTex
cloudMat.dispose();  // disposes cloudTex
sunTex.dispose();    // DOUBLE-DISPOSE of sunTex
moonTex.dispose();   // DOUBLE-DISPOSE of moonTex
cloudTex.dispose();  // DOUBLE-DISPOSE of cloudTex
```

**Suggested Fix:** 
Remove the explicit texture.dispose() calls, or alternatively don't dispose the materials and only dispose the textures manually.

---

### BUG #3: Double-Disposal of Precipitation Texture (HIGH CONFIDENCE, MEDIUM SEVERITY)

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/precipitation.ts`, lines 57-62 and 116-121

**Issue:**
Same as Bug #2 - PointsMaterial has a texture map that gets disposed twice

**Code Snippet:**
```typescript
const material = new THREE.PointsMaterial({ map: texture, ... }); // line 58
// In dispose():
material.dispose();  // disposes texture
texture.dispose();   // DOUBLE-DISPOSE
```

**Suggested Fix:** 
Remove the explicit texture.dispose() call.

---

### BUG #4: Active WebAudio Sources Not Cleaned Up on Dispose (HIGH CONFIDENCE, HIGH SEVERITY)

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/audio/synth.ts`, lines 28-93

**Issue:**
- When audio sounds are playing, they create BufferSource, Gain, and StereoPanner nodes
- The `dispose()` function (lines 88-91) only sets `disposed = true` and clears the buffer cache
- It does NOT stop or disconnect currently-playing sources
- These active nodes will continue playing their audio until they naturally end, and they won't be properly cleaned up

**Code Snippet:**
```typescript
export function createSynthBackend(ctx: AudioContext, ...): SynthBackend {
  let activeVoices = 0;
  
  return {
    play(def, opts = {}) {
      // ... creates source, gain, panner nodes
      source.onended = () => {
        activeVoices -= 1;
        source.disconnect();
        gain.disconnect();
        panner.disconnect();
      };
      source.start();
    },
    
    dispose() {
      disposed = true;
      buffers.clear();
      // MISSING: No cleanup of active sources/gains/panners!
      // If there are currently playing sounds, they won't be stopped
    }
  };
}
```

**Suggested Fix:**
Track all active source groups and explicitly stop/disconnect them in dispose():
```typescript
const activeVoices = new Set<() => void>(); // track cleanups
dispose() {
  disposed = true;
  for (const cleanup of activeVoices) cleanup();
  buffers.clear();
}
```

---

### BUG #5: React State Updates After Unmount from flashMessage Timeouts (MEDIUM CONFIDENCE, MEDIUM SEVERITY)

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, lines 173-176 and 291-306

**Issue:**
- The `flashMessage` function creates setTimeout callbacks that call `setSaveMessage("")`
- These timeouts are not tracked or cleared in the effect cleanup
- If the component unmounts before the timeout fires (e.g., switching worlds quickly, closing the browser tab), React will attempt a state update on an unmounted component
- This causes warnings in development and potential memory leaks (the timeout closure holds references to setSaveMessage)

**Code Snippet:**
```typescript
const flashMessage = useCallback((text: string, durationMs = 1200) => {
  setSaveMessage(text);
  window.setTimeout(() => setSaveMessage(""), durationMs);  // NOT cleaned up!
}, []);

useEffect(() => {
  // ... effect code calls flashMessage multiple times from the render loop
  if (event.type === "breakBlocked") {
    flashMessage("Not enough room to empty the chest");  // line 261
  }
  // ... more flashMessage calls
  
  return () => {
    // ... cleanup does NOT cancel pending setTimeout callbacks
    cancelAnimationFrame(animationFrame);
    window.clearInterval(autoSaveId);
    // Missing: tracking and clearing setTimeout IDs from flashMessage
  };
}, [ctx, flashMessage]);
```

**Suggested Fix:**
Track all setTimeout IDs created by flashMessage and clear them in the cleanup function:
```typescript
const timeoutIds = new Set<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>();

const flashMessage = useCallback((text: string, durationMs = 1200) => {
  setSaveMessage(text);
  const id = window.setTimeout(() => setSaveMessage(""), durationMs);
  timeoutIds.add(id);
}, []);

return () => {
  for (const id of timeoutIds) window.clearTimeout(id);
  // ... rest of cleanup
};
```

---

### SUMMARY

Found **5 distinct bugs**:
- 3 double-disposal bugs in Three.js materials/textures (Medium severity, High confidence)
- 1 resource leak in WebAudio node cleanup on dispose (High severity, High confidence)  
- 1 React state update after unmount (Medium severity, Medium confidence)

All bugs are real correctness issues that could cause crashes, memory leaks, or development warnings. The double-disposal issues are particularly concerning because Three.js may throw errors when disposing an already-disposed resource, especially in production builds or certain browser environments. The WebAudio leak is serious because it can cause audio to continue playing after the game is unmounted.

## Bug 1: Missing validation for player position on load

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`
**Line:** 217
**Severity:** HIGH
**Confidence:** HIGH

**Issue:**
Player position is loaded directly from the save without validation:
```typescript
if (save.player) this.state.player.position.set(save.player.x, save.player.y, save.player.z);
```

If a corrupted save has NaN or Infinity values in player coordinates, they are copied directly into the game state. Unlike other restorable fields (hearts, hunger, spawnPoint) which have dedicated validation functions, player position has none.

**Why this matters:**
- If player.y is NaN, the stuck-detection check `state.player.position.y < 2` at line 221 returns false (any comparison with NaN returns false), disabling the safety relocate
- NaN/Infinity in position could break collision detection and mob AI distance calculations
- A single corrupted byte in localStorage could permanently break a save until manually cleared

**Expected behavior:**
Player position should be validated the same way `restoreSpawnPoint` validates coordinates - checking that x, y, z are all finite numbers. There should be a `restorePlayerPosition` function.

**Suggested fix:**
Add a `restorePlayerPosition` function in save.ts similar to `restoreSpawnPoint`:
```typescript
export function restorePlayerPosition(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {
  const p = save.player;
  if (!p || !Number.isFinite(p.x) || !Number.isFinite(p.y) || !Number.isFinite(p.z)) return null;
  return { x: p.x, y: p.y, z: p.z };
}
```

Then in GameEngine.ts line 217, use it with a null check.

---

## Bug 2: No handling for localStorage quota exceeded errors

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts`
**Line:** 127-129
**Severity:** MEDIUM
**Confidence:** HIGH

**Issue:**
The `writeSave` function doesn't handle `QuotaExceededError` from localStorage:
```typescript
export function writeSave(saveKey: string, data: SaveData, storage: Storage = localStorage): void {
  storage.setItem(saveKey, JSON.stringify(data));
}
```

When localStorage quota is exceeded, `setItem` throws `QuotaExceededError`. The caller's try-catch in `persistGame` (useMinecraftGame.ts line 73-78) catches it but treats it as a generic save failure with no recovery mechanism.

**Why this matters:**
- The save is silently lost with only a "Save failed" message
- Player is unaware that their world is no longer being saved
- Continued gameplay after quota exhaustion could lead to a 15-30 second stretch of unautosaved progress being lost

**Suggested fix:**
1. Enhance `writeSave` to distinguish quota errors from other errors
2. Add specific handling in `persistGame` to warn the user about quota, potentially offering to:
   - Delete old autosaves
   - Reduce save frequency
   - Clear other localStorage data

Or at minimum, log quota errors differently so users know it's not a transient network issue.

---

## Bug 3: Serialize always writes empty arrays for optional fields

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`
**Line:** 486-488
**Severity:** LOW
**Confidence:** MEDIUM

**Issue:**
The `serialize()` method always writes empty arrays for optional fields:
```typescript
blockEntities: serializeContainers(state.containers),      // [] when no chests
lootedChests: serializeLootedChests(state.lootedDungeonChests),  // [] when no looted chests
effects: serializeEffects(state.effects),                   // [] when no effects
```

According to save-format.md, these fields are "optional" and should be absent (undefined) when empty, not empty arrays. However, the restore functions handle both cases correctly.

**Why this matters:**
- Saves are slightly larger than necessary (every save includes `,"blockEntities":[],"lootedChests":[],"effects":[]` even when unnecessary)
- Over time with many autosaves, this adds up
- Violates the documented schema: "Only **non-empty** chests are stored"

**Impact:** Minimal - this is more of a specification compliance issue than a correctness bug.

**Suggested fix:**
Modify serialize() to conditionally include these fields only when non-empty:
```typescript
...(blockEntities.length > 0 && { blockEntities }),
...(lootedChests.length > 0 && { lootedChests }),
...(effects.length > 0 && { effects }),
```

The restore functions already handle undefined correctly.

> AGENT

Confirmed my suspicion: in Three.js, `material.dispose()` does **not** dispose textures — so skyView and precipitation dispose material + texture separately (correct, *not* a double-dispose; those two findings are false). But GameRenderer's block-atlas texture (line 131, shared into `glassMaterial.map`) is **never** disposed — a real (minor) texture leak. Let me verify the remaining engine/audio/save candidates.

141	export function tickSpawnerDirector(state: GameState, dt: number, rng: () => number, emit: EmitGameEvent): void {
142	  if (state.dungeonSpawnerIndices.size === 0) return;
143	  state.timers.spawnerTimer += dt;
144	  if (state.timers.spawnerTimer < SPAWNER_INTERVAL_SECONDS) return;
145	  state.timers.spawnerTimer = 0;
146	
147	  const countHostiles = () => state.mobs.reduce((acc, mob) => acc + (mob.hostile ? 1 : 0), 0);
148	  if (countHostiles() >= HOSTILE_CAP) return;
149	
150	  const { player, world } = state;
151	  const layer = world.sizeX * world.sizeZ;
152	  for (const idx of state.dungeonSpawnerIndices) {
153	    const sy = Math.floor(idx / layer);
154	    const rem = idx - sy * layer;
155	    const sz = Math.floor(rem / world.sizeX);
156	    const sx = rem - sz * world.sizeX;
157	    if (world.get(sx, sy, sz) !== BlockId.Spawner) continue; // mined out → inert
158	    if (Math.hypot(player.position.x - (sx + 0.5), player.position.y - (sy + 0.5), player.position.z - (sz + 0.5)) > SPAWNER_ACTIVATION_RADIUS) continue;
159	    if (countHostiles() >= HOSTILE_CAP) break;
160	
161	    const nearby = state.mobs.reduce(
162	      (acc, mob) => acc + (mob.hostile && Math.hypot(mob.position.x - (sx + 0.5), mob.position.z - (sz + 0.5)) <= SPAWNER_ACTIVATION_RADIUS ? 1 : 0),
163	      0
164	    );
165	    if (nearby >= SPAWNER_LOCAL_CAP) continue;
166	
167	    const kind = SPAWNER_KINDS[Math.floor(rng() * SPAWNER_KINDS.length)];
168	    const jx = sx + 0.5 + (rng() * 2 - 1) * 1.2;
169	    const jz = sz + 0.5 + (rng() * 2 - 1) * 1.2;
170	    pushMob(state, kind, true, jx, sy, jz, rng);
171	    emit({ type: "mobSpawned", kind, x: sx + 0.5, y: sy + 0.5, z: sz + 0.5 });
172	  }
173	}

23	/** ZZFX.buildSamples signature, injected so this module never imports zzfx. */
24	export type BuildSamples = (...parameters: ZzfxParams) => number[];
25	
26	const MAX_VOICES = 10;
27	
28	export function createSynthBackend(ctx: AudioContext, destination: AudioNode, buildSamples: BuildSamples, sampleRate = 44100): SynthBackend {
29	  // Buffers are rendered once per SoundDef with randomness stripped; per-play
30	  // pitch variation comes from playbackRate jitter instead, so the cache stays
31	  // valid and play() never re-synthesizes.
32	  const buffers = new Map<SoundDef, AudioBuffer | null>();
33	  const lastPlayedAt = new Map<SoundDef, number>();
34	  let activeVoices = 0;
35	  let disposed = false;
36	
37	  const bufferFor = (def: SoundDef): AudioBuffer | null => {
38	    const cached = buffers.get(def);
39	    if (cached !== undefined) return cached;
40	    const params = [...def.params];
41	    params[1] = 0; // strip baked-in randomness; play() jitters playbackRate instead
42	    const samples = buildSamples(...params);
43	    let buffer: AudioBuffer | null = null;
44	    if (samples.length > 0) {
45	      buffer = ctx.createBuffer(1, samples.length, sampleRate);
46	      buffer.getChannelData(0).set(samples);
47	    }
48	    buffers.set(def, buffer);
49	    return buffer;
50	  };
51	
52	  return {
53	    now: () => ctx.currentTime,
54	
55	    play(def, opts = {}) {
56	      if (disposed || activeVoices >= MAX_VOICES) return;
57	      const now = ctx.currentTime;
58	      const retriggerSec = (def.minRetriggerMs ?? 0) / 1000;
59	      const last = lastPlayedAt.get(def);
60	      if (last !== undefined && now - last < retriggerSec) return;
61	
62	      const buffer = bufferFor(def);
63	      if (!buffer) return;
64	      lastPlayedAt.set(def, now);
65	
66	      const randomness = def.params[1] ?? 0.05;
67	      const jitter = 1 + (Math.random() * 2 - 1) * randomness;
68	
69	      const source = ctx.createBufferSource();
70	      source.buffer = buffer;
71	      source.playbackRate.value = (opts.rate ?? 1) * jitter;
72	      const gain = ctx.createGain();
73	      gain.gain.value = opts.gain ?? 1;
74	      const panner = ctx.createStereoPanner();
75	      panner.pan.value = Math.max(-1, Math.min(1, opts.pan ?? 0));
76	
77	      source.connect(gain).connect(panner).connect(destination);
78	      activeVoices += 1;
79	      source.onended = () => {
80	        activeVoices -= 1;
81	        source.disconnect();
82	        gain.disconnect();
83	        panner.disconnect();
84	      };
85	      source.start();
86	    },
87	
88	    dispose() {
89	      disposed = true;
90	      buffers.clear();
91	    }
92	  };
93	}
94

Findings refined. The **creeper** finding is false — there's already a `!isDead` guard at line 243 and the comment documents "killing it before the fuse runs out cancels it." The **spawn-director distance** mix (3D for activation, 2D for clustering) is a deliberate design choice, not a bug. Let me verify the persistence and React-lifecycle candidates, which look more promising.

233	  step(dt: number, input: FrameInput): void {
234	    const state = this.state;
235	    if (state.paused) {

1	import { CHEST_SLOTS, ENCHANT_MAX_LEVEL, HOTBAR_SLOTS, INVENTORY_SLOTS, MAX_HEARTS, MAX_HUNGER, MAX_STACK_SIZE } from "@/lib/game/config";
2	import { ARMOR_SLOTS, createEmptyArmorEquipment, createEmptySlot, createSlot, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID, maxStackSizeForItem } from "@/lib/game/items";
3	import type {
4	  EffectId,
5	  Enchantment,
6	  EnchantmentId,
7	  EquippedArmor,
8	  SaveData,
9	  SaveDataV1,
10	  SaveDataV2,
11	  SaveDataV3,
12	  SaveDataV4,
13	  SaveDataV5,
14	  SaveDataV6,
15	  SavedContainer,
16	  SavedEffect,
17	  SavedSlot,
18	  InventorySlot
19	} from "@/lib/game/types";
20	
21	/**
22	 * Migrates a v1 save (40 slots, 10-slot hotbar) to v2 (36 slots, 9-slot
23	 * hotbar): non-empty slots are packed in order, stackable items merge into
24	 * earlier stacks, and anything that still overflows 36 slots is dropped.
25	 */
26	/** Coerces a persisted hotbar index to a finite integer within 0..HOTBAR_SLOTS-1. */
27	function normalizeSelectedSlot(value: number): number {
28	  const index = Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.floor(value) : 0;
29	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(HOTBAR_SLOTS - 1, index));
30	}
31	
32	export function migrateSaveV1toV2(save: SaveDataV1): SaveDataV2 {
33	  const migrated: SaveDataV2 = { ...save, version: 2, selectedSlot: normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot) };
34	
35	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
36	    const packed: Array<{ id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number }> = [];
37	    for (const saved of save.inventorySlots) {
38	      if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0) continue;
39	      // Items without durability stack; merge them into an earlier stack first.
40	      const stackable = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id] ? !ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id].maxDurability : false;
41	      let remaining = saved.count;
42	      if (stackable) {
43	        for (const slot of packed) {
44	          if (slot.id !== saved.id || slot.count >= MAX_STACK_SIZE) continue;
45	          const moved = Math.min(MAX_STACK_SIZE - slot.count, remaining);
46	          slot.count += moved;
47	          remaining -= moved;
48	          if (remaining === 0) break;
49	        }
50	      }
51	      while (remaining > 0 && packed.length < INVENTORY_SLOTS) {
52	        const moved = Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(saved.id), remaining);
53	        packed.push({ id: saved.id, count: moved, durability: saved.durability });
54	        remaining -= moved;
55	      }
56	    }
57	    migrated.inventorySlots = packed;
58	  }
59	
60	  return migrated;
```

I've finished a verification pass. Here's the honest result — verifying mattered, because most of the explorers' flashy claims didn't survive scrutiny:

**Verified real (worth fixing):**
- **Player position isn't validated on load** — `GameEngine.ts:217` does `position.set(save.player.x/y/z)` with no finiteness check, unlike its sibling helpers (`restoreSpawnPoint`, `restoreDayClock`). A corrupted save with `NaN`/`Infinity` coords slips past the auto-unstuck net too (`NaN < 2` is `false`), so the world loads broken. Cheap fix matching the existing `restore*` pattern.
- **Block-atlas texture leak** — `GameRenderer.ts:131` creates the atlas texture and shares it into `glassMaterial.map`. On `dispose()`, the two materials are disposed but the texture never is (in Three.js `material.dispose()` does *not* dispose its map). One texture leaks per renderer teardown (i.e. each world switch).

**Minor / optional:**
- **Stray `setTimeout`s not cleared on unmount** — `flashMessage` (`useMinecraftGame.ts:175`) and the reload timers fire `setSaveMessage("")` after possible unmount. React 18 tolerates this, so it's hygiene, not a real bug.

**Claimed but FALSE (don't fix):**
- "Double-dispose" in `skyView`/`precipitation` — false; they correctly dispose material *and* texture (Three.js needs both).
- Creeper detonating after being killed — false; already guarded by `!isDead` (line 243).
- Synth not stopping voices on dispose — moot; `audioDirector.ts:124` calls `ctx.close()` immediately after, which stops everything.
- Spawn-director distance metric — deliberate design choice, not a bug.

Now — you mentioned you found something yourself. Let me fold that in before I finalize the plan.

## CRAFTING SYSTEM MAPPING REPORT

### 1. RECIPE DATA MODEL

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 60-71)

```typescript
export type Recipe = {
  id: string;
  label: string;
  cost: Array<{ slotId: string; count: number }>;
  result: { slotId: string; count: number };
  station?: "furnace" | "villager" | "brewing";
};
```

**Recipe definitions storage:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/recipes.ts` (lines 6-549)
- `CRAFTING_RECIPES` array: 61 base recipes (lines 6-549)
- Combined with `TRADES` from `lib/game/trades.ts` to form the complete `RECIPES` export (line 553)

**Example recipes:**
- Simple crafting: `{ id: "planks", label: "2 Wood -> 4 Planks", cost: [{ slotId: "wood", count: 2 }], result: { slotId: "planks", count: 4 } }` (line 7)
- Multi-ingredient: `{ id: "bed", label: "3 Wool + 3 Planks -> Bed", cost: [{ slotId: "wool", count: 3 }, { slotId: "planks", count: 3 }], result: { slotId: "bed", count: 1 } }` (lines 10-17)
- Station-gated (furnace): `{ id: "charcoal", label: "1 Wood -> 1 Charcoal", cost: [{ slotId: "wood", count: 1 }], result: { slotId: "charcoal", count: 1 }, station: "furnace" }` (line 76)
- Station-gated (brewing): `{ id: "potion_speed", label: "Glass Bottle + Feather -> Potion of Swiftness", cost: [{ slotId: "empty_bottle", count: 1 }, { slotId: "feather", count: 1 }], result: { slotId: "potion_speed", count: 1 }, station: "brewing" }` (lines 182-191)
- Villager trades: `{ id: "trade_wheat", label: "6 Wheat -> 1 Emerald", cost: [{ slotId: "wheat", count: 6 }], result: { slotId: "emerald", count: 1 }, station: "villager" }` (/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/trades.ts, line 15)

**Recipe categorization:**
- Type: `RecipeCategory = "Tools" | "Weapons" | "Armor" | "Building" | "Food" | "Materials" | "Smelting" | "Brewing" | "Trades"`
- Mapping function: `recipeCategory(recipe)` (lines 581-587) — station recipes get priority (`villager` → "Trades", `furnace` → "Smelting", `brewing` → "Brewing"), otherwise derives from item's `kind`
- Display order: `RECIPE_CATEGORY_ORDER` (line 565) — fixed sequence enforced by `groupRecipes()` (lines 596-615)

---

### 2. CRAFTING UI COMPONENTS

**Primary component:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`

**Key sections:**
- **Lines 12-61:** Props interface — includes `recipes`, `craftingStation`, `canCraft`, `onCraft`
- **Lines 112-117:** Crafting state logic:
  - `stationLocked(recipe)`: Returns `true` if recipe requires a station not currently open
  - `canMakeNow(recipe)`: Returns `true` only if BOTH unlocked AND ingredients available
  - `recipeGroups`: Output of `groupRecipes(recipes, canMakeNow)` — partitions recipes by category and sorts craftable ones to top
- **Lines 119-141:** `renderRecipe()` function — the critical render path:
  ```typescript
  const renderRecipe = (recipe: Recipe) => {
    const locked = stationLocked(recipe);
    return (
      <button
        key={recipe.id}
        className="recipe-entry"
        onClick={() => onCraft(recipe)}
        disabled={locked || !canCraft(recipe)}  // DISABLED if locked OR can't afford
        aria-label={recipe.label}
        {...bind({ title: recipe.label, lines: locked ? [`Requires ${STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]}`] : undefined })}
      >
        <span className="recipe-ingredients">
          {recipe.cost.map((cost) => (
            <ItemIcon key={`${recipe.id}-${cost.slotId}`} slot={createSlot(cost.slotId, cost.count)} size={24} />
          ))}
        </span>
        <span className="recipe-arrow" aria-hidden>→</span>
        <ItemIcon slot={createSlot(recipe.result.slotId, recipe.result.count)} size={24} />
      </button>
    );
  };
  ```
- **Lines 198-210:** Recipe book rendering:
  ```jsx
  <div className="recipe-book">
    <div className="inventory-heading">Crafting/Trading/Brewing</div>
    <div className="recipe-list">
      {recipeGroups.map((group) => (
        <Fragment key={group.category}>
          <div className="recipe-category">{group.category}</div>
          {group.recipes.map(renderRecipe)}
        </Fragment>
      ))}
    </div>
  </div>
  ```

**Current UX behavior when recipe is uncraftable (line 126):**
- Button gets `disabled` attribute
- Content still renders (ingredients, arrow, result)
- **BUT** styled as visually "disabled" — opacity reduced, images grayscale
- Tooltip shows only the recipe label, NOT the reason why (line 128: `lines: locked ? [...]` handles ONLY station-lock case, not ingredient shortage)
- Click handler `onClick={() => onCraft(recipe)}` is neutered by `disabled` attribute, so nothing happens

---

### 3. INGREDIENT AVAILABILITY CHECK

**Primary function:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/inventory.ts`, lines 123-135

```typescript
export function canCraft(slots: InventorySlot[], recipe: Recipe): boolean {
  const byId = countsById(slots);
  const hasCost = recipe.cost.every((cost) => (byId.get(cost.slotId) ?? 0) >= cost.count);
  if (!hasCost) return false;

  let freeForResult = 0;
  const stackSize = maxStackSizeForItem(recipe.result.slotId);
  for (const slot of slots) {
    if (slot.id === recipe.result.slotId) freeForResult += stackSize - slot.count;
    if (slot.id === null && slot.count === 0) freeForResult += stackSize;
  }
  return freeForResult >= recipe.result.count;
}
```

**Helper function:** `countsById()` (lines 16-23) — aggregates item counts across all slots by item ID

**Signature:** `canCraft(slots: InventorySlot[], recipe: Recipe): boolean`

**Two-part check:**
1. **Ingredient availability:** For each cost entry, verify total count across all slots >= required count
2. **Result space:** Verify inventory has room for the output (either existing stacks of the result item or empty slots)

**Called by:**
- InventoryPanel props: `canCraft` is passed in from the parent game component
- Inside `renderRecipe()` at line 126: `disabled={locked || !canCraft(recipe)}`
- Inside `craft()` function (line 144): Validates before consuming cost

---

### 4. "HOW TO OBTAIN" DATA

**Existing source data is scattered across loot tables — all located in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/`:**

#### A. Block drops (when mined)
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/items.ts` (lines 294-355)
- **Structure:** `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` map (lines 294-329) — maps `BlockId` → item ID
- **Special logic:** `rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng)` (lines 337-354) handles:
  - Leaves → sapling (by chance: `LEAVES_SAPLING_DROP_CHANCE`)
  - Grass → seeds (by chance: `GRASS_SEED_DROP_CHANCE`)
  - Mature wheat → wheat + 1-2 seeds
  - Doors → door item
- **Example:** `[BlockId.CoalOre]: "coal"` (line 306) — coal ore drops coal directly

#### B. Mob drops (when killed)
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/mobLoot.ts` (lines 20-65)
- **Structure:** `MOB_DROPS` record mapping mob kind → array of drop entries
- **Drop entry type:** `MobDrop = { itemId: string; min: number; max: number; chance?: number }`
- **Examples:**
  - Sheep: `wool` (1-2), `raw_mutton` (1) (lines 21-24)
  - Skeleton: `bone` (1-2) (line 36)
  - Creeper: `gunpowder` (1-2) (line 38)
  - Boss: `dragon_heart` (1), `diamond_ore` (2-4) (lines 42-45)

#### C. Fishing loot (caught with fishing rod)
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/fishingLoot.ts` (lines 16-27)
- **Structure:** `FISHING_LOOT` array of weighted entries
- **Entry type:** `FishingLootEntry = { itemId: string; weight: number; min?: number; max?: number }`
- **Examples:**
  - Raw Fish: weight 68 (most common) (line 18)
  - Junk (String, Bone, Seeds, Rotten Flesh): weights 5-8 (lines 20-23)
  - Treasure (Emerald, Leather): weight 3 each (lines 25-26)

#### D. Dungeon chest loot (found in generated dungeons)
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/dungeonLoot.ts` (lines 19-40)
- **Structure:** Two-tier system
  - `common` tier (always rolls): Bone, Bread, Cooked Chicken, String, Seeds, Sliver Ore, Gold Ore, Stone Pickaxe
  - `rare` tier (25% chance on top of common): Diamond Ore, Sapphire Ore, Ruby Sword, Sapphire Sword, Helmet, Chestplate, Diamond Pickaxe
- **Entry type:** `LootEntry = MobDrop` (same as mob drops)

#### E. Crafted from other recipes
- The recipes themselves form a dependency graph — examine `RECIPES` array to find recipes that produce each item

**Feasibility assessment for (c) "hint at how to obtain missing ingredients":**
- **YES, feasible from existing data.** For any missing ingredient itemId:
  - Check if it's in a recipe's result → player can craft it
  - Check `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` inverse → if it drops from a block, say "Mine [block name]"
  - Check `MOB_DROPS` inverse → if dropped by a mob, say "Kill [mob]"
  - Check `FISHING_LOOT` → if fishable, say "Fish"
  - Check `DUNGEON_LOOT` → if in dungeon chests, say "Find in dungeon"
  - Combine multiple sources if an item comes from several places

**Data structure needed:** A `getItemSources(itemId: string): ItemSource[]` function could be built that reads these tables and returns an array of sources. Each source would encode the type (block, mob, fish, dungeon, recipe) and enough detail to render a hint.

1	"use client";
2	
3	import { type MouseEvent, useCallback, useState } from "react";
4	import { createPortal } from "react-dom";
5	import type { InventorySlot } from "@/lib/game/types";
6	
7	export type TooltipContent = { title: string; lines?: string[] } | null;
8	
9	/**
10	 * The tooltip content for an inventory slot, shared by the hotbar and the
11	 * inventory so both surfaces show the same details: the item label, plus a gray
12	 * "Durability x / y" line for damageable items. Empty slots get no tooltip.
13	 */
14	export function itemTooltipFor(slot: InventorySlot): TooltipContent {
15	  if (!slot.id || slot.count <= 0) return null;
16	  if (slot.maxDurability) {
17	    const lines = [`Durability ${slot.durability ?? slot.maxDurability} / ${slot.maxDurability}`];
18	    if (slot.attack) lines.push(`Attack ${slot.attack}`);
19	    if (slot.meleeReach) lines.push(`Reach ${slot.meleeReach}`);
20	    if (slot.throwDamage) lines.push(`Throw damage ${slot.throwDamage}`);
21	    return { title: slot.label, lines };
22	  }
23	  return { title: slot.label };
24	}
25	
26	/**
27	 * Minecraft-style item tooltip: a near-black box with a violet gradient border
28	 * that follows the cursor (matching the game and sidestepping clipping inside
29	 * the scrollable inventory). It renders in a portal on document.body and is
30	 * pointer-events:none, so it can never intercept the canvas click that
31	 * re-acquires pointer lock. During locked play the cursor is hidden and slots
32	 * aren't hover-reachable, so the tooltip simply never shows.
33	 *
34	 * Usage: `const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();` then spread
35	 * `{...bind(content)}` onto each hoverable element and render `{tooltip}` once.
36	 * Pass `null` content for empty/non-hover targets to disable the tooltip there.
37	 * Keep each element's aria-label — the tooltip is aria-hidden.
38	 */
39	export function useItemTooltip() {
40	  const [content, setContent] = useState<TooltipContent>(null);
41	  const [pos, setPos] = useState({ x: 0, y: 0 });
42	
43	  const track = useCallback((e: MouseEvent) => setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY }), []);
44	
45	  const bind = useCallback(
46	    (c: TooltipContent) =>
47	      c
48	        ? {
49	            onMouseEnter: (e: MouseEvent) => {
50	              setContent(c);
51	              setPos({ x: e.clientX, y: e.clientY });
52	            },
53	            onMouseMove: track,
54	            onMouseLeave: () => setContent(null)
55	          }
56	        : {},
57	    [track]
58	  );
59	
60	  let tooltip: React.ReactNode = null;
61	  if (content && typeof document !== "undefined") {
62	    // Flip to the cursor's left near the right edge so wide tooltips stay on screen.
63	    const flip = typeof window !== "undefined" && pos.x > window.innerWidth - 300;
64	    const style = flip ? { right: window.innerWidth - pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 } : { left: pos.x + 14, top: pos.y + 14 };
65	    tooltip = createPortal(
66	      <div className="item-tooltip" style={style} aria-hidden="true">
67	        <span className="item-tooltip-title">{content.title}</span>
68	        {content.lines?.map((line) => (
69	          <span key={line} className="item-tooltip-line">
70	            {line}
71	          </span>
72	        ))}
73	      </div>,
74	      document.body
75	    );
76	  }
77	
78	  return { tooltip, bind };
79	}
80

1	import { MAX_STACK_SIZE } from "@/lib/game/config";
2	import { ARMOR_SLOTS, createEmptySlot, createSlot, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID, maxStackSizeForItem } from "@/lib/game/items";

6	/**
7	 * Pure inventory slot algebra. Every mutation returns a NEW slots array, or
8	 * null when the operation does not apply — callers keep the previous array on
9	 * null, so reference equality doubles as a change signal for React.
10	 */
11	
12	export function cloneSlots(slots: InventorySlot[]): InventorySlot[] {
13	  return slots.map((slot) => ({ ...slot }));
14	}
15	
16	export function countsById(slots: InventorySlot[]): Map<string, number> {
17	  const byId = new Map<string, number>();
18	  for (const slot of slots) {
19	    if (!slot.id || slot.count <= 0) continue;
20	    byId.set(slot.id, (byId.get(slot.id) ?? 0) + slot.count);
21	  }
22	  return byId;
23	}
24	
25	/**
26	 * Adds (delta > 0) or removes (delta < 0) items. Removal is all-or-nothing:
27	 * if the inventory holds fewer than |delta|, nothing is removed and null is
28	 * returned. Addition fills existing stacks, then empty slots; whatever does
29	 * not fit is dropped (a full inventory loses surplus block drops).
30	 */

555	// --- Recipe categories (recipe-book grouping) ---
556	
557	/**
558	 * The section a recipe falls under in the recipe book. Station recipes get their
559	 * own group (a furnace smelts, a villager trades); everything else is grouped by
560	 * the kind of item it produces.
561	 */
562	export type RecipeCategory = "Tools" | "Weapons" | "Armor" | "Building" | "Food" | "Materials" | "Smelting" | "Brewing" | "Trades";
563	
564	/** Fixed display order; `groupRecipes` emits sections in this sequence. */
565	export const RECIPE_CATEGORY_ORDER: RecipeCategory[] = ["Tools", "Weapons", "Armor", "Building", "Food", "Materials", "Smelting", "Brewing", "Trades"];
566	
567	const KIND_TO_CATEGORY: Record<ItemKind, RecipeCategory> = {
568	  tool: "Tools",
569	  weapon: "Weapons",
570	  armor: "Armor",
571	  block: "Building",
572	  food: "Food",
573	  material: "Materials"
574	};
575	
576	/**
577	 * The recipe book section for a recipe. Station recipes take precedence so
578	 * smelting and trades stay together; otherwise the category is derived from the
579	 * result item's `kind` (falling back to "Materials" for an unknown result).
580	 */
581	export function recipeCategory(recipe: Recipe): RecipeCategory {
582	  if (recipe.station === "villager") return "Trades";
583	  if (recipe.station === "furnace") return "Smelting";
584	  if (recipe.station === "brewing") return "Brewing";
585	  const kind = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId]?.kind;
586	  return kind ? KIND_TO_CATEGORY[kind] : "Materials";
587	}
588	
589	export type RecipeGroup = { category: RecipeCategory; recipes: Recipe[] };
590	
591	/**
592	 * Buckets recipes into the fixed category order, dropping empty categories.
593	 * Within each group, recipes the player can make right now (`canMakeNow`) are
594	 * listed first; both partitions preserve the original source order.
595	 */
596	export function groupRecipes(recipes: Recipe[], canMakeNow: (recipe: Recipe) => boolean): RecipeGroup[] {
597	  const buckets = new Map<RecipeCategory, Recipe[]>();
598	  for (const recipe of recipes) {
599	    const category = recipeCategory(recipe);
600	    const bucket = buckets.get(category);
601	    if (bucket) bucket.push(recipe);
602	    else buckets.set(category, [recipe]);
603	  }
604	
605	  const groups: RecipeGroup[] = [];
606	  for (const category of RECIPE_CATEGORY_ORDER) {
607	    const bucket = buckets.get(category);
608	    if (!bucket) continue;
609	    const ready: Recipe[] = [];
610	    const rest: Recipe[] = [];
611	    for (const recipe of bucket) (canMakeNow(recipe) ? ready : rest).push(recipe);
612	    groups.push({ category, recipes: [...ready, ...rest] });
613	  }
614	  return groups;
615	}
616

39	/**

41	 * a recipe book where every entry shows ingredient icons and the result.
42	 * Items move by clicking one slot and then another (click-to-swap); clicking
43	 * an armor item toggles equipping it instead.

45	export default function InventoryPanel({

48	  selectedHotbarSlot,

62	  const [pendingIndex, setPendingIndex] = useState<number | null>(null);
63	  const { tooltip, bind } = useItemTooltip();
64	
65	  // While a chest is open, moves span both grids (chest slots are offset by
66	  // CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE); otherwise a swap stays within the player inventory.
67	  const slotAt = (index: number): InventorySlot | undefined => (index >= CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE ? container?.[index - CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE] : inventory[index]);

69	  const onSlotClick = (index: number) => {
70	    const slot = slotAt(index);

73	    if (index < CONTAINER_SLOT_BASE && slot.kind === "armor" && slot.count > 0) {
74	      onToggleEquipArmor(index);
75	      setPendingIndex(null);

> AGENT

I now have a complete, verified picture. One important discovery while tracing the UI: the recipe rows use the native `disabled` attribute, and **disabled buttons don't fire hover events in browsers** — so the hover tooltip never appears on uncraftable recipes (the existing "Requires Furnace" hint is silently broken too). That's exactly why "no recipe is displayed" on hover. The fix will swap `disabled` for `aria-disabled` + a guarded `onClick`, which restores hover and lets us show details.

The content you want is clear (show recipe, show what's missing, show how to obtain). The one open design choice is the presentation surface, which changes the implementation footprint — let me confirm that.

New module — `lib/game/itemSources.ts` (\"how to obtain\")\nA pure lookup `itemSourceHint(itemId: string): string | null` returning one short hint, built from\ninverse maps computed **once at module load** from the existing static tables:\n- **Craft** — scan `RECIPES` (`recipes.ts:553`) for `result.slotId === itemId`; phrase by station\n  (`furnace`→\"smelt it\", `villager`→\"trade for it\", `brewing`→\"brew it\", else \"craft it\").\n- **Mine** — invert `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` (`items.ts:294`); \"mine <block label>\" (block label via the item\n  whose `blockId` matches). Add a small explicit map for `rollBlockDrops` special cases\n  (`items.ts:337`): sapling←leaves, seeds←grass, wheat←mature wheat.\n- **Hunt** — invert `MOB_DROPS` (`mobLoot.ts`); \"hunt <mob>\" (mob kind → label via a small title-case map).\n- **Fish** — `FISHING_LOOT` (`fishingLoot.ts`); \"catch it while fishing\".\n- **Dungeon** — `DUNGEON_LOOT` (`dungeonLoot.ts`); \"find it in a dungeon chest\".\n\nPriority order Craft > Mine > Hunt > Fish > Dungeon; return `null` if unknown (then omit the line).\nItem display labels come from `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[id]?.label` (`items.ts`).\n\n### 1c. UI rewire — `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx` (`renderRecipe`, lines 119–141)\n- Compute `const craftable = !locked && canCraft(recipe)`.\n- **Stop using native `disabled`.** Replace `disabled={...}` with `aria-disabled={!craftable}`, add a\n  conditional class (e.g. `recipe-entry${craftable ? \"\" : \" is-disabled\"}`), and guard the handler:\n  `onClick={() => { if (craftable) onCraft(recipe); }}`. This restores hover events so the tooltip fires.\n- Build enriched tooltip content (new local helper `recipeTooltip(recipe, inventory, locked)`):\n  - `title`: `recipe.label`.\n  - If `locked`: first line `Requires <STATION_LABELS[...]>` (existing behavior, now actually visible).\n  - Else if `!craftable`: one line per ingredient from `ingredientStatus(inventory, recipe)` —\n    `\"<label>  <have> / <need>\"`, appending `\"(need N more)\"` when short; then, for each short\n    ingredient, a hint line `\"→ <label>: <itemSourceHint(slotId)>\"` (skip if hint is `null`).\n  - If `craftable`: keep minimal (title only), matching current behavior.\n- `inventory` prop is the authoritative slot array — `canCraft` in the parent is\n  `inv.canCraft(snapshot.inventory, recipe)` (`useMinecraftGame.ts:356`), same slots.\n\n### 1d. CSS — `app/ui.css` (lines 466–477)\nSwap the `:disabled` selectors for the class, preserving the exact look:\n- `.recipe-entry:disabled` → `.recipe-entry.is-disabled` (cursor `not-allowed`, `opacity: 0.45`).\n- `.recipe-entry:disabled img` → `.recipe-entry.is-disabled img` (`filter: grayscale(0.9)`).\n- `.recipe-entry:hover:not(:disabled)` → `.recipe-entry:hover:not(.is-disabled)`.\n\n---\n\n## Part 2 — Validate player position on load\n\n- `lib/game/save.ts`: add `restorePlayerPosition(save): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null`,\n  mirroring `restoreSpawnPoint` (line 326) — return `null` unless `save.player` exists and `x/y/z` are\n  all `Number.isFinite`.\n- `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts:217`: replace the direct set with\n  ```ts\n  const pos = restorePlayerPosition(save);\n  if (pos) this.state.player.position.set(pos.x, pos.y, pos.z);\n  ```\n  Invalid/missing data now leaves the default spawn in place, which the existing unstuck net\n  (line 221) then validates — no broken NaN world.\n\n---\n\n## Part 3 — Fix block-atlas texture leak\n\n`lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`:\n- Add field `private readonly blockAtlasTexture: THREE.Texture;`.\n- Line 131: `const tex = createBlockAtlasTexture(); this.blockAtlasTexture = tex;` and use `map: tex`\n  for `worldMaterial`; line 138 `map: tex` for `glassMaterial` (drop `this.worldMaterial.map`).\n- In `dispose()` (after lines 549–550): `this.blockAtlasTexture.dispose();` — disposed once\n  (Three.js `material.dispose()` does **not** dispose the map; the shared texture is otherwise leaked).\n\n---\n\n## Part 4 — Clear stray `setTimeout`s on unmount (minor)\n\n`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`:\n- Add `const timeoutsRef = useRef(new Set<number>());`.\n- In `flashMessage` (line 173): track the id, and clear it from the set when it fires.\n- Track the two reload timers (lines 378, 385) the same way.\n- Add a top-level `useEffect(() => () => { for (const id of timeoutsRef.current) clearTimeout(id); timeoutsRef.current.clear(); }, [])` to clear all pending timers on unmount.\n\n---\n\n## Tests (ship with the change)\n\n- `lib/game/inventory.test.ts` — `ingredientStatus`: have<need, have≥need, missing item (have 0).\n- `lib/game/itemSources.test.ts` (new) — `itemSourceHint` for a craftable item, a mined block item,\n  a mob-drop item, a fished item, a dungeon-only item, and an unknown id (`null`).\n- `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx` — uncraftable recipe renders `aria-disabled=\"true\"`\n  (not native `disabled`), `onCraft` not fired on click, and the hover tooltip lists missing\n  ingredient have/need + an obtain hint; station-locked recipe shows the \"Requires …\" line.\n- `lib/game/save.test.ts` — `restorePlayerPosition` returns coords for a valid save and `null` for\n  NaN/Infinity/missing; engine load with a NaN-position save does not place the player at NaN.\n\n## Docs & changelog (part of the gate)\n\n- `docs/manual.md` — crafting section: the recipe book now shows, on hover of an unaffordable recipe,\n  what you're missing and how to get it.\n- `docs/reference.md` — note the new behavior if it documents the recipe book UI.\n- `docs/adding-content.md` — new item/recipe walkthrough: mention `itemSources.ts` derives the\n  \"how to obtain\" hint from the loot tables automatically (and how to extend it for special drops).\n- `docs/save-format.md` — note player position is finiteness-validated on load.\n- `docs/testing.md` — add the new tests to the coverage map.\n- `CHANGELOG.md` — one entry covering the crafting hints + the three fixes.\n\n## Verification (end-to-end)\n\n1. `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build` — all green.\n2. `bun run test:e2e` (Part 1/3 touch renderer + React shell).\n3. Manual pass: open the inventory, hover a recipe you can't afford → tooltip shows ingredient\n   have/need and a \"how to obtain\" line; the row is unclickable but hoverable; a furnace/brewing\n   recipe shows \"Requires …\". Confirm craftable recipes still craft on click. Hand-edit a save in\n   localStorage to put `NaN` in `player.y`, reload → player spawns sanely instead of a broken world.\n"

> AGENT

The plan is written. Here's the summary before you approve:

**Headline (your finding) — Crafting hints via enriched tooltip.** Root cause found: recipe rows use the native `disabled` attribute, which suppresses hover events in browsers — so the tooltip never shows (the existing "Requires Furnace" hint is broken the same way). Fix swaps to `aria-disabled` + a guarded `onClick`, then enriches the hover tooltip with per-ingredient **have/need**, flags what's short, and adds a "→ how to obtain" line per missing item. A new `itemSources.ts` derives those hints (craft / mine / hunt / fish / dungeon) from the loot tables that already exist — no new data needed.

**Three verified fixes you selected:**
- Player-position finiteness check on load (mirrors `restoreSpawnPoint`).
- Block-atlas texture leak — dispose the shared texture once on teardown.
- Stray `setTimeout` cleanup on unmount.

**Excluded** — several flashy explorer claims that failed verification (Three.js "double-dispose", creeper detonation race, synth voice leak, spawn-director distance) — noted in the plan so we don't waste effort on non-bugs.

All four ship with tests, doc updates, and a CHANGELOG entry, on a `feat/crafting-hints-and-fixes` branch in focused commits.

## Approved Plan:
# Improvements & Bugfixes — Crafting Hints + 3 Verified Fixes

## Context

A collaborative improvement pass. The headline change is a crafting UX request from the user:
uncraftable recipes in the inventory render grayed-out and give **no hover feedback** about
*what* is missing or *how* to get it. While tracing this, I found the root cause: recipe rows use
the native `disabled` attribute, and **disabled buttons don't fire hover events in browsers**, so
the tooltip never appears (the existing "Requires Furnace" station hint is silently broken for the
same reason). We'll fix the mechanism and enrich the tooltip to show per-ingredient have/need plus a
short "how to obtain" hint per missing item.

Bundled with it are three issues I verified by reading the code (a larger explorer sweep produced
several claims that did **not** survive verification — e.g. Three.js "double-dispose", a creeper
detonation race, synth voice leak — those are intentionally excluded):

1. **Player position not validated on load** — robustness bug; NaN/Infinity coords from a corrupt
   save load a broken world and bypass the auto-unstuck net (`NaN < 2` is `false`).
2. **Block-atlas texture leak** — the world atlas texture is never disposed on renderer teardown.
3. **Stray `setTimeout`s not cleared on unmount** — minor React hygiene.

Branch: feature-scoped work → new branch `feat/crafting-hints-and-fixes` off `main`, focused commits
(one per concern). Verify gate must be green before it lands.

---

## Part 1 — Crafting: show recipe + missing ingredients + how-to-obtain (enriched tooltip)

### 1a. New pure helper — `lib/game/inventory.ts`
Add alongside `countsById` (line 16) and `canCraft` (line 123):
```ts
export type IngredientStatus = { slotId: string; have: number; need: number };

/** Per-ingredient have-vs-need for a recipe, in cost order. have < need ⇒ short. */
export function ingredientStatus(slots: InventorySlot[], recipe: Recipe): IngredientStatus[] {
  const byId = countsById(slots);
  return recipe.cost.map((c) => ({ slotId: c.slotId, have: byId.get(c.slotId) ?? 0, need: c.count }));
}
```

### 1b. New module — `lib/game/itemSources.ts` ("how to obtain")
A pure lookup `itemSourceHint(itemId: string): string | null` returning one short hint, built from
inverse maps computed **once at module load** from the existing static tables:
- **Craft** — scan `RECIPES` (`recipes.ts:553`) for `result.slotId === itemId`; phrase by station
  (`furnace`→"smelt it", `villager`→"trade for it", `brewing`→"brew it", else "craft it").
- **Mine** — invert `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` (`items.ts:294`); "mine <block label>" (block label via the item
  whose `blockId` matches). Add a small explicit map for `rollBlockDrops` special cases
  (`items.ts:337`): sapling←leaves, seeds←grass, wheat←mature wheat.
- **Hunt** — invert `MOB_DROPS` (`mobLoot.ts`); "hunt <mob>" (mob kind → label via a small title-case map).
- **Fish** — `FISHING_LOOT` (`fishingLoot.ts`); "catch it while fishing".
- **Dungeon** — `DUNGEON_LOOT` (`dungeonLoot.ts`); "find it in a dungeon chest".

Priority order Craft > Mine > Hunt > Fish > Dungeon; return `null` if unknown (then omit the line).
Item display labels come from `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[id]?.label` (`items.ts`).

### 1c. UI rewire — `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx` (`renderRecipe`, lines 119–141)
- Compute `const craftable = !locked && canCraft(recipe)`.
- **Stop using native `disabled`.** Replace `disabled={...}` with `aria-disabled={!craftable}`, add a
  conditional class (e.g. `recipe-entry${craftable ? "" : " is-disabled"}`), and guard the handler:
  `onClick={() => { if (craftable) onCraft(recipe); }}`. This restores hover events so the tooltip fires.
- Build enriched tooltip content (new local helper `recipeTooltip(recipe, inventory, locked)`):
  - `title`: `recipe.label`.
  - If `locked`: first line `Requires <STATION_LABELS[...]>` (existing behavior, now actually visible).
  - Else if `!craftable`: one line per ingredient from `ingredientStatus(inventory, recipe)` —
    `"<label>  <have> / <need>"`, appending `"(need N more)"` when short; then, for each short
    ingredient, a hint line `"→ <label>: <itemSourceHint(slotId)>"` (skip if hint is `null`).
  - If `craftable`: keep minimal (title only), matching current behavior.
- `inventory` prop is the authoritative slot array — `canCraft` in the parent is
  `inv.canCraft(snapshot.inventory, recipe)` (`useMinecraftGame.ts:356`), same slots.

### 1d. CSS — `app/ui.css` (lines 466–477)
Swap the `:disabled` selectors for the class, preserving the exact look:
- `.recipe-entry:disabled` → `.recipe-entry.is-disabled` (cursor `not-allowed`, `opacity: 0.45`).
- `.recipe-entry:disabled img` → `.recipe-entry.is-disabled img` (`filter: grayscale(0.9)`).
- `.recipe-entry:hover:not(:disabled)` → `.recipe-entry:hover:not(.is-disabled)`.

---

## Part 2 — Validate player position on load

- `lib/game/save.ts`: add `restorePlayerPosition(save): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null`,
  mirroring `restoreSpawnPoint` (line 326) — return `null` unless `save.player` exists and `x/y/z` are
  all `Number.isFinite`.
- `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts:217`: replace the direct set with
  ```ts
  const pos = restorePlayerPosition(save);
  if (pos) this.state.player.position.set(pos.x, pos.y, pos.z);
  ```
  Invalid/missing data now leaves the default spawn in place, which the existing unstuck net
  (line 221) then validates — no broken NaN world.

---

## Part 3 — Fix block-atlas texture leak

`lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts`:
- Add field `private readonly blockAtlasTexture: THREE.Texture;`.
- Line 131: `const tex = createBlockAtlasTexture(); this.blockAtlasTexture = tex;` and use `map: tex`
  for `worldMaterial`; line 138 `map: tex` for `glassMaterial` (drop `this.worldMaterial.map`).
- In `dispose()` (after lines 549–550): `this.blockAtlasTexture.dispose();` — disposed once
  (Three.js `material.dispose()` does **not** dispose the map; the shared texture is otherwise leaked).

---

## Part 4 — Clear stray `setTimeout`s on unmount (minor)

`lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`:
- Add `const timeoutsRef = useRef(new Set<number>());`.
- In `flashMessage` (line 173): track the id, and clear it from the set when it fires.
- Track the two reload timers (lines 378, 385) the same way.
- Add a top-level `useEffect(() => () => { for (const id of timeoutsRef.current) clearTimeout(id); timeoutsRef.current.clear(); }, [])` to clear all pending timers on unmount.

---

## Tests (ship with the change)

- `lib/game/inventory.test.ts` — `ingredientStatus`: have<need, have≥need, missing item (have 0).
- `lib/game/itemSources.test.ts` (new) — `itemSourceHint` for a craftable item, a mined block item,
  a mob-drop item, a fished item, a dungeon-only item, and an unknown id (`null`).
- `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx` — uncraftable recipe renders `aria-disabled="true"`
  (not native `disabled`), `onCraft` not fired on click, and the hover tooltip lists missing
  ingredient have/need + an obtain hint; station-locked recipe shows the "Requires …" line.
- `lib/game/save.test.ts` — `restorePlayerPosition` returns coords for a valid save and `null` for
  NaN/Infinity/missing; engine load with a NaN-position save does not place the player at NaN.

## Docs & changelog (part of the gate)

- `docs/manual.md` — crafting section: the recipe book now shows, on hover of an unaffordable recipe,
  what you're missing and how to get it.
- `docs/reference.md` — note the new behavior if it documents the recipe book UI.
- `docs/adding-content.md` — new item/recipe walkthrough: mention `itemSources.ts` derives the
  "how to obtain" hint from the loot tables automatically (and how to extend it for special drops).
- `docs/save-format.md` — note player position is finiteness-validated on load.
- `docs/testing.md` — add the new tests to the coverage map.
- `CHANGELOG.md` — one entry covering the crafting hints + the three fixes.

## Verification (end-to-end)

1. `bun run lint`, `bun run typecheck`, `bun run format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build` — all green.
2. `bun run test:e2e` (Part 1/3 touch renderer + React shell).
3. Manual pass: open the inventory, hover a recipe you can't afford → tooltip shows ingredient
   have/need and a "how to obtain" line; the row is unclickable but hoverable; a furnace/brewing
   recipe shows "Requires …". Confirm craftable recipes still craft on click. Hand-edit a save in
   localStorage to put `NaN` in `player.y`, reload → player spawns sanely instead of a broken world.

```

294	export const BLOCK_TO_SLOT: Partial<Record<BlockId, string>> = {
295	  [BlockId.Grass]: "grass",
296	  [BlockId.Dirt]: "dirt",
297	  [BlockId.Stone]: "stone",
298	  [BlockId.Wood]: "wood",
299	  // Leaves drop only a sapling (by chance) — handled in rollBlockDrops, not here.
300	  [BlockId.Planks]: "planks",
301	  [BlockId.Cobblestone]: "cobble",
302	  [BlockId.Sand]: "sand",
303	  [BlockId.Brick]: "brick",
304	  [BlockId.Glass]: "glass",
305	  // Coal ore drops the usable coal item directly (not a placeable ore block).
306	  [BlockId.CoalOre]: "coal",
307	  [BlockId.SliverOre]: "sliver_ore",
308	  [BlockId.RubyOre]: "ruby_ore",
309	  [BlockId.GoldOre]: "gold_ore",
310	  [BlockId.SapphireOre]: "sapphire_ore",
311	  [BlockId.DiamondOre]: "diamond_ore",
312	  [BlockId.Snow]: "snow",
313	  [BlockId.Cactus]: "cactus",
314	  [BlockId.Bed]: "bed",
315	  [BlockId.Furnace]: "furnace",
316	  [BlockId.Chest]: "chest",
317	  [BlockId.BrewingStand]: "brewing_stand",
318	  [BlockId.EnchantingTable]: "enchanting_table",
319	  [BlockId.MossyCobblestone]: "mossy_cobble",
320	  [BlockId.Torch]: "torch",
321	  [BlockId.Tnt]: "tnt",
322	  [BlockId.Sapling]: "sapling",
323	  [BlockId.DoorNorthLower]: "door",
324	  // Tilled soil reverts to dirt; immature wheat returns its seed.
325	  [BlockId.Farmland]: "dirt",
326	  [BlockId.WheatStage0]: "seeds",
327	  [BlockId.WheatStage1]: "seeds",
328	  [BlockId.WheatStage2]: "seeds"
329	};
330	
331	/**
332	 * Items a broken block yields. The default is its single `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` entry;
333	 * grass occasionally also drops a seed (the natural seed source), mature wheat
334	 * drops wheat plus 1–2 seeds, and leaves occasionally drop a sapling (their only
335	 * yield — the renewable tree source). `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests.
336	 */
337	export function rollBlockDrops(block: BlockId, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
338	  if (isDoorBlock(block)) return [{ itemId: "door", count: 1 }];
339	  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
340	  const base = BLOCK_TO_SLOT[block];
341	  if (base) drops.push({ itemId: base, count: 1 });
342	
343	  if (block === BlockId.Leaves && rng() < LEAVES_SAPLING_DROP_CHANCE) {
344	    drops.push({ itemId: "sapling", count: 1 });
345	  }
346	  if (block === BlockId.Grass && rng() < GRASS_SEED_DROP_CHANCE) {
347	    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 });
348	  }
349	  if (block === BlockId.WheatStage3) {
350	    drops.push({ itemId: "wheat", count: 1 });
351	    drops.push({ itemId: "seeds", count: 1 + Math.floor(rng() * 2) });
352	  }
353	  return drops;
354	}
355

48	/** Clamps an rng sample into [0, 1) so a pathological injected rng can't over-roll. */
49	const clampUnit = (v: number): number => Math.min(1 - Number.EPSILON, Math.max(0, v));

51	/**
52	 * Rolls the drop table for a mob kind. Returns one entry per item that yielded a
53	 * positive count; the engine adds each to the inventory. `rng` is injectable so
54	 * tests get deterministic counts (0 → min, ~1 → max).
55	 */
56	export function rollMobDrops(kind: MobKind, rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
57	  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
58	  for (const entry of MOB_DROPS[kind]) {
59	    if (entry.chance !== undefined && clampUnit(rng()) >= entry.chance) continue;
60	    const span = entry.max - entry.min + 1;
61	    const count = entry.min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * span);
62	    if (count > 0) drops.push({ itemId: entry.itemId, count });
63	  }
64	  return drops;
65	}
66

1	/**
2	 * The fishing catch table: one weighted pick per bite. Mostly raw fish, sometimes
3	 * junk (string/bone/seeds/rotten flesh), rarely a treasure (emerald/leather).
4	 * Weights are relative — summed at roll time — so adding an entry only reweights
5	 * the odds. Mirrors the other loot rollers' output shape so a catch is added to
6	 * the inventory the same way (`adjustSlotCount`).
7	 */
8	
9	export type FishingLootEntry = {
10	  itemId: string;
11	  weight: number;
12	  min?: number;
13	  max?: number;
14	};
15	
16	export const FISHING_LOOT: FishingLootEntry[] = [
17	  // Fish — the bulk of every catch table.
18	  { itemId: "raw_fish", weight: 68, min: 1, max: 1 },
19	  // Junk.
20	  { itemId: "string", weight: 8 },
21	  { itemId: "bone", weight: 7 },
22	  { itemId: "seeds", weight: 6 },
23	  { itemId: "rotten_flesh", weight: 5 },
24	  // Treasure (rare).
25	  { itemId: "emerald", weight: 3 },
26	  { itemId: "leather", weight: 3 }
27	];
28	
29	const clampUnit = (v: number): number => (v < 0 ? 0 : v > 1 ? 1 : v);
30	
31	/** One weighted pick from FISHING_LOOT. `rng` is injectable for deterministic tests. */
32	export function rollFishingCatch(rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
33	  const total = FISHING_LOOT.reduce((sum, entry) => sum + entry.weight, 0);
34	  let roll = clampUnit(rng()) * total;
35	  for (const entry of FISHING_LOOT) {
36	    roll -= entry.weight;
37	    if (roll < 0) {
38	      const min = entry.min ?? 1;
39	      const max = entry.max ?? 1;
40	      const count = min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * (max - min + 1));
41	      return [{ itemId: entry.itemId, count }];
42	    }
43	  }
44	  // Floating-point guard: a roll landing exactly at the total falls to the last entry.
45	  const last = FISHING_LOOT[FISHING_LOOT.length - 1];
46	  return [{ itemId: last.itemId, count: last.min ?? 1 }];
47	}
48

1	import type { MobDrop } from "@/lib/game/mobLoot";
2	
3	/**
4	 * Dungeon chest loot, mirroring the mob/block drop-table pattern
5	 * (`{ itemId, min, max, chance? }` entries rolled for an inclusive count).
6	 *
7	 * A chest rolls a tier first: most are `common` (staples — food, low ores, a
8	 * starter tool), a minority are `rare` and additionally roll the standout
9	 * payoff table (high ores, mid-tier gear). Rare chests still get the common
10	 * roll on top, so they are never *worse* than a common chest. Item ids must
11	 * exist in ITEM_DEFS — dungeonLoot.test.ts enforces that.
12	 */
13	export type LootEntry = MobDrop;
14	export type LootTier = "common" | "rare";
15	
16	/** Probability a chest rolls the rare tier (on top of the common roll). */
17	export const RARE_CHEST_CHANCE = 0.25;
18	
19	export const DUNGEON_LOOT: Record<LootTier, LootEntry[]> = {
20	  common: [
21	    // Bone always drops, so every dungeon chest holds at least something.
22	    { itemId: "bone", min: 1, max: 3 },
23	    { itemId: "bread", min: 1, max: 3, chance: 0.8 },
24	    { itemId: "cooked_chicken", min: 1, max: 2, chance: 0.5 },
25	    { itemId: "string", min: 0, max: 2 },
26	    { itemId: "seeds", min: 0, max: 2 },
27	    { itemId: "sliver_ore", min: 1, max: 3, chance: 0.4 },
28	    { itemId: "gold_ore", min: 1, max: 3, chance: 0.4 },
29	    { itemId: "stone_pickaxe", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.25 }
30	  ],
31	  rare: [
32	    { itemId: "diamond_ore", min: 1, max: 2, chance: 0.3 },
33	    { itemId: "sapphire_ore", min: 1, max: 2, chance: 0.35 },
34	    { itemId: "ruby_sword", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.15 },
35	    { itemId: "sapphire_sword", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.15 },
36	    { itemId: "helmet", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.2 },
37	    { itemId: "chestplate", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.2 },
38	    { itemId: "diamond_pickaxe", min: 1, max: 1, chance: 0.08 }
39	  ]
40	};
41	
42	/** Clamps an rng sample into [0, 1) so a pathological injected rng can't over-roll. */
43	const clampUnit = (v: number): number => Math.min(1 - Number.EPSILON, Math.max(0, v));
44	
45	/**
46	 * A small mulberry32 PRNG. Dungeon chests are filled lazily on first open, so
47	 * the loot must be reproducible from a stable seed (the world seed mixed with
48	 * the chest's voxel index) until the chest is actually opened — otherwise the
49	 * same chest would roll different loot before vs after a reload.
50	 */
51	export function seededRng(seed: number): () => number {
52	  let t = seed >>> 0;
53	  return () => {
54	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
55	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
56	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
57	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
58	  };
59	}
60	
61	function rollEntries(entries: LootEntry[], rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
62	  const drops: Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> = [];
63	  for (const entry of entries) {
64	    if (entry.chance !== undefined && clampUnit(rng()) >= entry.chance) continue;
65	    const span = entry.max - entry.min + 1;
66	    const count = entry.min + Math.floor(clampUnit(rng()) * span);
67	    if (count > 0) drops.push({ itemId: entry.itemId, count });
68	  }
69	  return drops;
70	}
71	
72	/**
73	 * Rolls one dungeon chest. Consumes one rng sample for the tier, then rolls the
74	 * common table (always) plus the rare table when the chest is rare. `rng` is
75	 * injectable so tests get deterministic loot (and the engine seeds it per chest).
76	 */
77	export function rollDungeonLoot(rng: () => number): Array<{ itemId: string; count: number }> {
78	  const rare = clampUnit(rng()) < RARE_CHEST_CHANCE;
79	  const drops = rare ? rollEntries(DUNGEON_LOOT.rare, rng) : [];
80	  drops.push(...rollEntries(DUNGEON_LOOT.common, rng));
81	  return drops;
82	}
83	
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/itemSources.ts",
  "content": "import { DUNGEON_LOOT } from \"@/lib/game/dungeonLoot\";\nimport { FISHING_LOOT } from \"@/lib/game/fishingLoot\";\nimport { BLOCK_TO_SLOT, ITEM_DEF_BY_ID } from \"@/lib/game/items\";\nimport { MOB_DROPS } from \"@/lib/game/mobLoot\";\nimport { RECIPES } from \"@/lib/game/recipes\";\nimport type { MobKind, Recipe } from \"@/lib/game/types\";\n\n/**\n * \"How to obtain\" hints for the recipe book: when a recipe is short an\n * ingredient, the tooltip tells the player where that item comes from. Hints are\n * derived once from the existing static loot/recipe tables — no new data — so a\n * new item or recipe is covered automatically (special block drops aside).\n */\n\n/** Verb for a craftable item, keyed by the station its recipe needs (\"none\" = plain crafting). */\nconst CRAFT_VERB: Record<\"none\" | NonNullable<Recipe[\"station\"]>, string> = {\n  none: \"Craft it\",\n  furnace: \"Smelt it\",\n  villager: \"Trade for it\",\n  brewing: \"Brew it\"\n};\n\n/** Display names for mobs, used in \"Hunt <mob>\" hints. */\nconst MOB_LABELS: Record<MobKind, string> = {\n  sheep: \"a sheep\",\n  chicken: \"a chicken\",\n  horse: \"a horse\",\n  cow: \"a cow\",\n  pig: \"a pig\",\n  zombie: \"a zombie\",\n  skeleton: \"a skeleton\",\n  spider: \"a spider\",\n  creeper: \"a creeper\",\n  villager: \"a villager\",\n  boss: \"the boss\"\n};\n\n/**\n * Overrides for items whose dropped form differs from the block you break, so the\n * generic \"Mine <item label>\" reads correctly (coal comes from coal ore;\n * saplings/seeds/wheat are chance drops handled in `rollBlockDrops`, not direct).\n */\nconst MINE_OVERRIDES: Record<string, string> = {\n  coal: \"Mine coal ore\",\n  sapling: \"Break leaves\",\n  seeds: \"Break grass\",\n  wheat: \"Harvest grown wheat\"\n};\n\nconst labelFor = (itemId: string): string => ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[itemId]?.label ?? itemId;\n\n/**\n * itemId → single best \"how to obtain\" hint, precomputed once from the static\n * tables. Priority: craft > mine > hunt > fish > dungeon, so the most direct,\n * reliable source wins (you hunt a cow for leather rather than fish for it).\n */\nconst SOURCE_HINTS: Map<string, string> = (() => {\n  const hints = new Map<string, string>();\n  const set = (itemId: string, hint: string): void => {\n    if (!hints.has(itemId)) hints.set(itemId, hint);\n  };\n\n  // 1. Craft (incl. smelt/trade/brew). The first recipe producing the item wins.\n  for (const recipe of RECIPES) set(recipe.result.slotId, CRAFT_VERB[recipe.station ?? \"none\"]);\n  // 2. Mine (direct block drops, then chance-drop overrides not in BLOCK_TO_SLOT).\n  for (const itemId of Object.values(BLOCK_TO_SLOT)) {\n    if (itemId) set(itemId, MINE_OVERRIDES[itemId] ?? `Mine ${labelFor(itemId)}`);\n  }\n  for (const [itemId, hint] of Object.entries(MINE_OVERRIDES)) set(itemId, hint);\n  // 3. Hunt (mob drops).\n  for (const [kind, drops] of Object.entries(MOB_DROPS)) {\n    for (const drop of drops) set(drop.itemId, `Hunt ${MOB_LABELS[kind as MobKind]}`);\n  }\n  // 4. Fish.\n  for (const entry of FISHING_LOOT) set(entry.itemId, \"Catch it while fishing\");\n  // 5. Dungeon chests.\n  for (const tier of Object.values(DUNGEON_LOOT)) {\n    for (const entry of tier) set(entry.itemId, \"Find it in a dungeon chest\");\n  }\n  return hints;\n})();\n\n/** A short, human \"how to obtain\" hint for an item, or null when its source is unknown. */\nexport function itemSourceHint(itemId: string): string | null {\n  return SOURCE_HINTS.get(itemId) ?? null;\n}\n"
}

123	export function canCraft(slots: InventorySlot[], recipe: Recipe): boolean {
124	  const byId = countsById(slots);
125	  const hasCost = recipe.cost.every((cost) => (byId.get(cost.slotId) ?? 0) >= cost.count);
126	  if (!hasCost) return false;
127	
128	  let freeForResult = 0;
129	  const stackSize = maxStackSizeForItem(recipe.result.slotId);
130	  for (const slot of slots) {
131	    if (slot.id === recipe.result.slotId) freeForResult += stackSize - slot.count;
132	    if (slot.id === null && slot.count === 0) freeForResult += stackSize;
133	  }
134	  return freeForResult >= recipe.result.count;
135	}
136	
137	/**
138	 * Consumes the recipe cost and adds the result. Returns null unless the cost
139	 * is affordable AND the result fits — crafting never silently destroys the
140	 * overflow (this previously could happen when the inventory was full).
141	 */
142	export function craft(slots: InventorySlot[], recipe: Recipe): InventorySlot[] | null {
143	  if (!ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[recipe.result.slotId]) return null;
144	  if (!canCraft(slots, recipe)) return null;
145	
146	  const next = cloneSlots(slots);
147	  for (const cost of recipe.cost) {
148	    let remaining = cost.count;
149	    for (let i = 0; i < next.length && remaining > 0; i += 1) {
150	      if (next[i].id !== cost.slotId || next[i].count <= 0) continue;
151	      const take = Math.min(remaining, next[i].count);
152	      next[i].count -= take;
153	      remaining -= take;
154	      if (next[i].count <= 0) next[i] = createEmptySlot();
155	    }
156	  }
157	
158	  let remaining = recipe.result.count;

}
