  "old_string": "const STATION_LABELS: Record<NonNullable<Recipe[\"station\"]>, string> = {\n  furnace: \"Furnace\",\n  villager: \"Villager\",\n  brewing: \"Brewing Stand\"\n};",
  "new_string": "const STATION_LABELS: Record<NonNullable<Recipe[\"station\"]>, string> = {\n  furnace: \"Furnace\",\n  villager: \"Villager\",\n  brewing: \"Brewing Stand\"\n};\n\nconst labelFor = (slotId: string): string => ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[slotId]?.label ?? slotId;\n\n/**\n * Hover text for a recipe. A craftable recipe just names itself; a station-locked\n * one says which station it needs; an unaffordable one lists each ingredient as\n * have/need and, for every shortfall, a \"how to obtain\" hint. The detail is what\n * the user can't read from the tiny grayscale icons alone.\n */\nfunction recipeTooltip(recipe: Recipe, inventory: InventorySlot[], locked: boolean, craftable: boolean): TooltipContent {\n  if (locked) return { title: labelFor(recipe.result.slotId), lines: [`Requires ${STATION_LABELS[recipe.station!]}`] };\n  if (craftable) return { title: recipe.label };\n\n  const status = ingredientStatus(inventory, recipe);\n  const lines = status.map((s) => `${labelFor(s.slotId)}  ${s.have} / ${s.need}${s.have < s.need ? ` (need ${s.need - s.have} more)` : \" ✓\"}`);\n  for (const s of status) {\n    if (s.have >= s.need) continue;\n    const hint = itemSourceHint(s.slotId);\n    if (hint) lines.push(`→ ${labelFor(s.slotId)}: ${hint}`);\n  }\n  return { title: labelFor(recipe.result.slotId), lines };\n}"
}

319	/** Restores banked XP (finite, ≥ 0); 0 when absent or invalid. */
320	export function restoreXp(save: SaveData): number {
321	  if (typeof save.xp !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.xp) || save.xp < 0) return 0;
322	  return Math.floor(save.xp);
323	}
324	
325	/** Restores the bed respawn point; null if absent or explicitly cleared. */
326	export function restoreSpawnPoint(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {
327	  const sp = save.spawnPoint;
328	  if (!sp || !Number.isFinite(sp.x) || !Number.isFinite(sp.y) || !Number.isFinite(sp.z)) return null;
329	  return { x: Math.floor(sp.x), y: Math.floor(sp.y), z: Math.floor(sp.z) };

  "old_string": "/** Restores the bed respawn point; null if absent or explicitly cleared. */\nexport function restoreSpawnPoint(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {\n  const sp = save.spawnPoint;\n  if (!sp || !Number.isFinite(sp.x) || !Number.isFinite(sp.y) || !Number.isFinite(sp.z)) return null;\n  return { x: Math.floor(sp.x), y: Math.floor(sp.y), z: Math.floor(sp.z) };\n}",
  "new_string": "/** Restores the bed respawn point; null if absent or explicitly cleared. */\nexport function restoreSpawnPoint(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {\n  const sp = save.spawnPoint;\n  if (!sp || !Number.isFinite(sp.x) || !Number.isFinite(sp.y) || !Number.isFinite(sp.z)) return null;\n  return { x: Math.floor(sp.x), y: Math.floor(sp.y), z: Math.floor(sp.z) };\n}\n\n/**\n * Restores the player's position; null when absent or non-finite. A corrupt save\n * with NaN/Infinity coords would otherwise load a broken world — and slip past the\n * `position.y < 2` unstuck net, since any comparison with NaN is false. Coords stay\n * floats (the player isn't grid-aligned), unlike the floored spawn point.\n */\nexport function restorePlayerPosition(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {\n  const p = save.player;\n  if (!p || !Number.isFinite(p.x) || !Number.isFinite(p.y) || !Number.isFinite(p.z)) return null;\n  return { x: p.x, y: p.y, z: p.z };\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "new_string": "  // Tracks each timer so unmount can cancel it; the timer also self-removes when\n  // it fires so the set never grows unbounded.\n  const scheduleTimeout = useCallback((fn: () => void, ms: number) => {\n    const id = window.setTimeout(() => {\n      pendingTimeouts.current.delete(id);\n      fn();\n    }, ms);\n    pendingTimeouts.current.add(id);\n  }, []);\n\n  useEffect(\n    () => () => {\n      for (const id of pendingTimeouts.current) window.clearTimeout(id);\n      pendingTimeouts.current.clear();\n    },\n    []\n  );\n\n  const flashMessage = useCallback(\n    (text: string, durationMs = 1200) => {\n      setSaveMessage(text);\n      scheduleTimeout(() => setSaveMessage(\"\"), durationMs);\n    },\n    [scheduleTimeout]\n  );"
}

10	
11	function makeInventory(...items: Array<[string, number] | null>): InventorySlot[] {
12	  const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
13	  items.forEach((item, i) => {
14	    if (item) slots[i] = createSlot(item[0], item[1]);
15	  });
16	  return slots;
17	}
18	
19	function renderPanel(overrides: Partial<Parameters<typeof InventoryPanel>[0]> = {}) {
20	  const props = {
21	    inventory: makeInventory(["dirt", 10], ["stone", 5], ["helmet", 1]),
22	    equippedArmor: createEmptyArmorEquipment(),
23	    selectedHotbarSlot: 0,
24	    hotbarSlots: HOTBAR_SLOTS,
25	    recipes: RECIPES,
26	    craftingStation: null as "furnace" | "enchanting" | null,
27	    container: null as InventorySlot[] | null,
28	    xpLevel: 0,
29	    enchantCost: 3,
30	    canCraft: () => true,
31	    onSwapSlots: mock(),
32	    onMoveStack: mock(),
33	    onToggleEquipArmor: mock(),
34	    onCraft: mock(),
35	    onEnchant: mock(),
36	    ...overrides
37	  };
38	  render(<InventoryPanel {...props} />);
39	  return props;
40	}
41	
42	function slotButtons() {
43	  // Inventory slot wells, excluding the armor column and recipe entries.
44	  return screen.getAllByRole("button").filter((button) => button.className.includes("inv-slot") && !button.className.includes("armor-slot"));
45	}
46	
47	function recipeOrder() {
48	  // The recipe entries in DOM order, identified by their aria-label (the recipe label).
49	  return screen
50	    .getAllByRole("button")
51	    .filter((button) => button.className.includes("recipe-entry"))
52	    .map((button) => button.getAttribute("aria-label"));
53	}

> AGENT

Important catch: wool **is** craftable (`wool_from_string`), so a naive craft-first priority would make wool say "Craft it" instead of the more natural "Hunt a sheep" — and a hunt-first priority would wrongly make diamond ore say "Hunt the boss." Let me reorder the priority thoughtfully: hunt common mobs first (excluding the one-off boss), then craft, mine, fish, dungeon, with the boss as a last resort for its trophy-only drops.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/itemSources.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * itemId → single best \"how to obtain\" hint, precomputed once from the static\n * tables. Priority: craft > mine > hunt > fish > dungeon, so the most direct,\n * reliable source wins (you hunt a cow for leather rather than fish for it).\n */\nconst SOURCE_HINTS: Map<string, string> = (() => {\n  const hints = new Map<string, string>();\n  const set = (itemId: string, hint: string): void => {\n    if (!hints.has(itemId)) hints.set(itemId, hint);\n  };\n\n  // 1. Craft (incl. smelt/trade/brew). The first recipe producing the item wins.\n  for (const recipe of RECIPES) set(recipe.result.slotId, CRAFT_VERB[recipe.station ?? \"none\"]);\n  // 2. Mine (direct block drops, then chance-drop overrides not in BLOCK_TO_SLOT).\n  for (const itemId of Object.values(BLOCK_TO_SLOT)) {\n    if (itemId) set(itemId, MINE_OVERRIDES[itemId] ?? `Mine ${labelFor(itemId)}`);\n  }\n  for (const [itemId, hint] of Object.entries(MINE_OVERRIDES)) set(itemId, hint);\n  // 3. Hunt (mob drops).\n  for (const [kind, drops] of Object.entries(MOB_DROPS)) {\n    for (const drop of drops) set(drop.itemId, `Hunt ${MOB_LABELS[kind as MobKind]}`);\n  }\n  // 4. Fish.\n  for (const entry of FISHING_LOOT) set(entry.itemId, \"Catch it while fishing\");\n  // 5. Dungeon chests.\n  for (const tier of Object.values(DUNGEON_LOOT)) {\n    for (const entry of tier) set(entry.itemId, \"Find it in a dungeon chest\");\n  }\n  return hints;\n})();",
  "new_string": "/**\n * itemId → single best \"how to obtain\" hint, precomputed once from the static\n * tables. Priority is chosen so each item points at its most natural, repeatable\n * source: hunt a common mob (not the one-off boss) → craft → mine → fish → dungeon\n * → boss. Hunting comes before crafting so a raw drop like wool says \"hunt a\n * sheep\" rather than the obscure craft-from-string path; the boss is last so its\n * drops (e.g. diamond ore) prefer mining and only surface the boss for its\n * trophy. The first source set for an item wins.\n */\nconst SOURCE_HINTS: Map<string, string> = (() => {\n  const hints = new Map<string, string>();\n  const set = (itemId: string, hint: string): void => {\n    if (!hints.has(itemId)) hints.set(itemId, hint);\n  };\n\n  // 1. Hunt (every mob except the boss — it isn't a farmable source).\n  for (const [kind, drops] of Object.entries(MOB_DROPS)) {\n    if (kind === \"boss\") continue;\n    for (const drop of drops) set(drop.itemId, `Hunt ${MOB_LABELS[kind as MobKind]}`);\n  }\n  // 2. Craft (incl. smelt/trade/brew). The first recipe producing the item wins.\n  for (const recipe of RECIPES) set(recipe.result.slotId, CRAFT_VERB[recipe.station ?? \"none\"]);\n  // 3. Mine (direct block drops, then chance-drop overrides not in BLOCK_TO_SLOT).\n  for (const itemId of Object.values(BLOCK_TO_SLOT)) {\n    if (itemId) set(itemId, MINE_OVERRIDES[itemId] ?? `Mine ${labelFor(itemId)}`);\n  }\n  for (const [itemId, hint] of Object.entries(MINE_OVERRIDES)) set(itemId, hint);\n  // 4. Fish.\n  for (const entry of FISHING_LOOT) set(entry.itemId, \"Catch it while fishing\");\n  // 5. Dungeon chests.\n  for (const tier of Object.values(DUNGEON_LOOT)) {\n    for (const entry of tier) set(entry.itemId, \"Find it in a dungeon chest\");\n  }\n  // 6. Boss (last resort — only its trophy drops with no other source reach here).\n  for (const drop of MOB_DROPS.boss) set(drop.itemId, \"Defeat the boss\");\n  return hints;\n})();"
}
```

22	function makeSlots(...items: Array<[string, number] | null>): InventorySlot[] {
23	  const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
24	  items.forEach((item, i) => {
25	    if (item) slots[i] = createSlot(item[0], item[1]);
26	  });
27	  return slots;
28	}

38	    expect(byId.get("stone")).toBe(5);
39	    expect(byId.has("")).toBe(false);
40	  });
41	});

describe("save round-trip", () => {
  test("writeSave then readSave preserves every field", () => {
    const storage = memoryStorage();
    writeSave(KEY, sampleSave(), storage);
    expect(readSave(KEY, storage)).toEqual(sampleSave());
  });

  test("legacy saves with inventoryCounts instead of inventorySlots still parse", () => {
    const legacy = {
      version: 1,
      seed: 7,
      changes: [],
      inventoryCounts: { dirt: 30, stone: 5 },
      selectedSlot: 0,
      player: { x: 1, y: 2, z: 3 }
    };
    const storage = memoryStorage({ [KEY]: JSON.stringify(legacy) });
    const parsed = readSave(KEY, storage);
    expect(parsed).not.toBeNull();
    expect(parsed!.version).toBe(7);
    expect(parsed!.inventoryCounts).toEqual({ dirt: 30, stone: 5 });
    expect(parsed!.inventorySlots).toBeUndefined();
  });

  "old_string": "  test(\"craft buttons are disabled per canCraft and click through onCraft\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const planks = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"planks\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ canCraft: (recipe) => recipe.id === \"planks\" });\n\n    const planksButton = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: planks.label }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    const glassButton = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: \"4 Sand -> 2 Glass\" }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    expect(planksButton.disabled).toBe(false);\n    expect(glassButton.disabled).toBe(true);\n\n    await user.click(planksButton);\n    expect(props.onCraft).toHaveBeenCalledWith(planks);\n  });\n\n  test(\"a furnace recipe is locked with no station open\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const cook = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"cook_chicken\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ craftingStation: null });\n    const button = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: cook.label }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    expect(button.disabled).toBe(true);\n    await user.click(button);\n    expect(props.onCraft).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  test(\"a furnace recipe unlocks when the furnace is open\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const cook = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"cook_chicken\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ craftingStation: \"furnace\" });\n    const button = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: cook.label }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    expect(button.disabled).toBe(false);\n    await user.click(button);\n    expect(props.onCraft).toHaveBeenCalledWith(cook);\n  });",
  "new_string": "  test(\"unaffordable recipes are aria-disabled and swallow the craft click; affordable ones fire\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const planks = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"planks\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ canCraft: (recipe) => recipe.id === \"planks\" });\n\n    // Not the native `disabled` attribute (it would suppress the hover tooltip);\n    // the row stays a live button but the handler no-ops when unaffordable.\n    const planksButton = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: planks.label }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    const glassButton = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: \"4 Sand -> 2 Glass\" }) as HTMLButtonElement;\n    expect(planksButton.disabled).toBe(false);\n    expect(planksButton.getAttribute(\"aria-disabled\")).toBe(\"false\");\n    expect(glassButton.getAttribute(\"aria-disabled\")).toBe(\"true\");\n    expect(glassButton.className).toContain(\"is-disabled\");\n\n    await user.click(planksButton);\n    expect(props.onCraft).toHaveBeenCalledWith(planks);\n    await user.click(glassButton); // unaffordable → guarded\n    expect(props.onCraft).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);\n  });\n\n  test(\"a furnace recipe is locked (aria-disabled) with no station open\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const cook = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"cook_chicken\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ craftingStation: null });\n    const button = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: cook.label });\n    expect(button.getAttribute(\"aria-disabled\")).toBe(\"true\");\n    await user.click(button);\n    expect(props.onCraft).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  test(\"a furnace recipe unlocks when the furnace is open\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const cook = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"cook_chicken\")!;\n    const props = renderPanel({ craftingStation: \"furnace\" });\n    const button = screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: cook.label });\n    expect(button.getAttribute(\"aria-disabled\")).toBe(\"false\");\n    await user.click(button);\n    expect(props.onCraft).toHaveBeenCalledWith(cook);\n  });\n\n  test(\"hovering an unaffordable recipe shows missing ingredients and how to obtain them\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const bed = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"bed\")!; // 3 wool + 3 planks\n    // Has the planks but no wool, and nothing is craftable.\n    renderPanel({ inventory: makeInventory([\"planks\", 3]), canCraft: () => false });\n\n    await user.hover(screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: bed.label }));\n    expect(screen.getByText(/^Wool\\s+0 \\/ 3 \\(need 3 more\\)$/)).toBeTruthy();\n    expect(screen.getByText(/^Planks\\s+3 \\/ 3 ✓$/)).toBeTruthy();\n    expect(screen.getByText(\"→ Wool: Hunt a sheep\")).toBeTruthy(); // the obtain hint\n  });\n\n  test(\"hovering a station-locked recipe explains which station it needs\", async () => {\n    const user = userEvent.setup();\n    const cook = RECIPES.find((recipe) => recipe.id === \"cook_chicken\")!;\n    renderPanel({ craftingStation: null });\n    await user.hover(screen.getByRole(\"button\", { name: cook.label }));\n    expect(screen.getByText(\"Requires Furnace\")).toBeTruthy();\n  });"
}

=== CHANGELOG top ===
# Changelog

All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **XP & enchanting**: the game gains its first progression currency and a way to spend it, all on **zero new assets** (a procedural XP bar + effect-coloured table sprite, synthesized blip/shimmer). The save schema bumps **v6 → v7** (additive), with **no worldgen change**
  - **XP** (`state.xp`, banked points; `XP_PER_LEVEL` points = one level): earned from **mob kills** (hostiles more than passives, the **boss a jackpot**; babies give none, like drops — table in `mobXp.ts`), **mining ores** (scaled by rarity), and **fishing** catches. A green **XP bar** above the hotbar fills toward the next level with the level number over it. Unlike status effects, XP is a long-term currency and is **not cleared on death**
  - **Enchanting**: craft an **Enchanting Table** (2 diamond ore + 4 cobble) and right-click it to open the enchanting panel, then spend XP levels to enchant the **selected** tool/weapon/armor. Deterministic and player-chosen (no random rolls): four enchantments, each up to **level 3** — **Sharpness** (+melee damage), **Protection** (−incoming damage), **Efficiency** (+mining speed), and **Unbreaking** (probabilistically saves durability). Each maps to exactly one existing seam, the same one-reader-per-seam discipline as status effects
  - **Architecture**: enchantments are **per-item-instance data, like durability** (non-stackable gear, slot-cloned on insert), so the inventory algebra and persistence are reused — they ride the existing `SavedSlot` snapshot/restore (validated on load). XP/level math + the award/spend helpers live in `lib/game/engine/systems/xp.ts`; the readers + `canEnchant`/`applyEnchant` in `lib/game/enchantments.ts`. Enchanting is a new `enchant` command (not a recipe — `craft` produces a fixed result and can't mutate the held instance). The `EnchantingTable` `BlockId` is appended (id 52) so existing saved block ids never shift; `xp` and per-slot `enchantments` are additive v7 fields, so pre-XP saves load clean
- **Status effects, brewing & potions**: the game gains its first timed-buff system — six effects, five of them brewed into potions at a new station, all on **zero new assets** (procedural flask/icon sprites + a synthesized "glug"). The save schema bumps **v5 → v6** (additive), with **no worldgen change**
  - **Effects** (`state.effects`, a Map of id → remaining seconds): **Speed** (×1.2 movement), **Strength** (+3 melee damage), **Regeneration** (heals on its own cadence, even at low hunger), **Fire Resistance** (lava can't burn you), **Water Breathing** (lungs stay full, no drowning), and **Poison** (chips you down but **never kills** — floors at half a heart). Each is read at exactly one existing seam (movement speed, attack damage, the lava/oxygen ticks, or a per-tick accumulator), so the simulation stays modular. Re-applying an effect refreshes to the longer remaining time; all are cleared on death
  - **Brewing Stand** (craft 3 cobble + 1 gold ore): a new interactive block — right-click to open its **Brewing** recipes, exactly like a furnace opens smelting, reusing the whole `station`-recipe pipeline. Glass bottles come first (3 glass → 3 bottles), then a bottle + one reagent brews a potion: **feather → Swiftness**, **gunpowder → Strength**, **wheat → Regeneration**, **coal → Fire Resistance**, **raw fish → Water Breathing** (all existing drops/crops, so no new worldgen). Drink with the **F** key (the eat key is potion-aware); drinking consumes the whole bottle
  - **Poison hazard**: eating **rotten flesh** has an 80% chance to poison you — a real downside to the most desperate food. Poison is the only effect with no potion
  - **HUD**: active effects show as a stacked top-left column of procedural icon + m:ss countdown, opposite the minimap, hidden when nothing is active. The projection is cached and rebuilt only when the rounded seconds change, so it re-renders ~1 Hz instead of every frame
  - **Architecture**: effects live in a new `lib/game/engine/systems/statusEffects.ts` (helpers + `tickStatusEffects`, which runs after `tickHealthRegen` and before the environmental ticks so the fire-resist/water-breathing gates are current). Poison uses a **new** `applyNonLethalDamage` rather than the killing `applyUnmitigatedDamage`. The `BrewingStand` `BlockId` is appended at the end of the enum (id 51) so existing saved block ids never shift. Effects are additive on save v6 (a pure version bump; pre-effect saves load with none) and **cleared on death**, so a saved-then-reloaded world resumes whatever was active
- **Dragon Lord tracker**: while the boss lives, its top-center health HUD now includes a rotating pointer toward it and its horizontal distance in blocks, making it possible to reacquire the fight after terrain or summoned mobs break line of sight. The values are rounded before entering the React snapshot to avoid sub-block render churn. This is transient UI state with **no save-format or worldgen impact**.
- **Fishing**: water was only ever a hazard to avoid — now it's a place to fish, a relaxed renewable food source with the odd lucky find, all on **zero new assets** (a procedural rod/fish sprite + synthesized splashes). **No save-format or worldgen change** — the cast is transient session state and the new items save by string id
  - **Fishing rod** (craft 3 wood + 2 string): right-click while aiming at water to **cast** a bobber. After a random wait it **dips with a splash** (a bite) — right-click again within ~1.2 s to **reel in** a catch; miss the window and it gets away while the bobber keeps fishing
  - **Catch table**: a weighted single pick per bite — mostly **raw fish** (cook it at a furnace, like the meats), sometimes junk (string/bone/seeds/rotten flesh), and rarely a treasure (emerald). Only a successful reel wears the rod
  - **Feedback**: a water-splash particle burst when a fish bites and again on the reel-in, plus the reel sound. Common fish/junk catches stay diegetic (splash + sound + the hotbar count) so frequent fishing never spams the HUD; only the rare **treasure** catch raises a one-line toast
  - **Rod animation**: the rod is no longer static — it winds up and flicks on a cast, holds out over the water while waiting (with a tip twitch on a bite), and yanks back on a reel, in **both first- and third-person**. The bobber **arcs out from the rod tip** to the water and **arcs back** on reel-in (the line is anchored at the rod tip, not the eye), and on a catch the caught item **flies from the bobber to the player**. All render-side — composed onto the pure pose pipeline (`heldItemPose.ts`/`playerPose.ts`) and driven by the fishing events + the `state.fishing` edges, so a silent cancel still reels the bobber back cleanly
  - **Architecture**: the cast is one session-only `state.fishing` field (a bobber position + a bite timer), never serialized and cleared on respawn — the same transient-entity pattern as arrows and thrown spears. A new water-aware `waterSurfaceRaycast` (`lib/world/queries.ts`) finds the surface to aim at, since the normal raycast passes through water. The bobber and a `THREE.Line` fishing line render by reading engine state directly (`lib/game/render/bobberVisuals.ts`); `tryFish`/`tickFishing` (`lib/game/engine/systems/fishing.ts`) drive cast/bite/reel and auto-cancel when the rod is unequipped, the player dies, the water drains, or they stray past the tether
- **Living world — saplings, bone meal & grass spread**: the world now renews and heals itself, all on **zero new assets** (a procedural sprout atlas tile + a recolored powder sprite). **No save-format or worldgen change** — every effect is an ordinary block edit riding the save diff, and the hash-pinned worldgen tree code is left untouched
  - **Saplings**: breaking **leaves** now drops a **sapling** (its only yield, ~8% per break) instead of the old placeholder dirt. Right-click a sapling onto grass/dirt to plant it; once it sits on soil it matures into a tree through the existing random-tick system (`SAPLING_GROWTH_CHANCE`). Trees are renewable at last
  - **Bone meal**: grind a skeleton's **bone** into **bone meal** (1 → 3, in the recipe book's Materials section), then right-click it on a sapling to grow the tree **instantly**, or on immature wheat to advance it a random **1–2** stages. A second, renewable use for bones beyond the cursed totem
  - **Grass spread**: exposed **dirt** (a column's top block) **re-grasses** over time when one of its four face-neighbour columns has grass on top, healing mined or built-over terrain
  - **Architecture**: tree building lives in `lib/game/engine/systems/treeGrowth.ts`, shared by the sapling random-tick handler and the bone-meal use; it writes through the `blockChanges` chokepoint (so the tree persists and relights) using the engine rng, and deliberately shares **no** code with worldgen's `placeTrees`, so the generation hashes stay **byte-identical**. Saplings render like wheat — a solid cube with an atlas-painted texture — so there is no new geometry path. The `Sapling` `BlockId` is appended at the end of the enum (id 50) so existing saved block ids never shift
=== manual.md crafting ===
3:A complete guide to playing Monecraft — a single-player, browser-based voxel
4:sandbox. Mine, build, craft, farm, fight off the night, and survive.

226	## Crafting
227	
228	Open the inventory (`I`) to reach the crafting panel. Pick a recipe whose
229	ingredients you have and craft it — the result drops into your inventory (crafting
230	is refused if there's no room rather than destroying the overflow). Stacks hold up
231	to **99** items; tools, weapons, and armor take one slot each and show durability.
232	
233	The recipe book is **grouped into labeled sections** — Tools, Weapons, Armor,
234	Building, Food, Materials, then the station-gated Smelting, Brewing, and Trades —
235	and within each section the recipes you can make **right now** float to the top, so
236	what's craftable is easy to spot among the dimmed entries.
237	
238	The basic recipe grid is always available. **Smelting** recipes (cooking raw meat)
239	require a **furnace**: craft one from 8 cobble, place it, and right-click it to open
240	the panel in furnace mode — the cooking recipes unlock while a furnace is open and
241	show as "Requires Furnace" otherwise.
242	
243	See the full list of **65 recipes** in the [reference](reference.md#recipes).
244	
245	## Brewing and potions
246	
247	Once you've gathered some gold, craft a **brewing stand** (3 cobble + 1 gold ore),
248	place it, and right-click it to open the panel in **brewing** mode — just like a
249	furnace, the potion recipes unlock while the stand is open.
250	
251	Every potion starts with a **glass bottle** (3 glass → 3 bottles, on the basic
252	grid). Add one reagent at the stand to brew a potion:
253	
254	| Potion          | Reagent   | While active                          |
255	| --------------- | --------- | ------------------------------------- |
```

3:Step-by-step recipes for extending the game. See [architecture.md](architecture.md) for how these pieces fit together.
5:## A new block
9:3. Make it placeable/droppable: an `ITEM_DEFS` entry (`kind: "block"`, `blockId`) plus a `BLOCK_TO_SLOT` mapping — without the mapping, mining it drops nothing. The inventory icon (isometric cube) auto-generates from `BLOCK_COLORS`; ore-style blocks can add an accent color in `lib/ui/spritePixels.ts` (`ORE_ACCENTS`).
10:4. Optionally add `RECIPES` entries in `lib/game/recipes.ts`.
14:8. The item/recipe integrity tests (`lib/game/config.test.ts`) will fail if a mapping is missing or inconsistent — run `bun test`.
16:## A new item or recipe
30:- Recipes are `{ id, label, cost: [{slotId, count}], result: {slotId, count} }` in `lib/game/recipes.ts`. An optional `station` makes a recipe station-gated: it only crafts while that station's panel is open, enforced in the `craft` command and shown locked in the recipe book. The stations are `"furnace"` (smelting), `"villager"` (a **trade** — see below), and `"brewing"` (potions, opened at a brewing stand). Adding a station means extending that union in **four** places (`Recipe.station` in `types.ts`, `craftingStation` on both `GameState` and `GameSnapshot` in `state.ts`, and the `openedStation` event), plus a `RecipeCategory` + a `recipeCategory()` branch and a `STATION_LABELS` entry (`InventoryPanel.tsx`).
31:- **Villager trades** (`lib/game/trades.ts`) ARE just `station: "villager"` recipes spread into `RECIPES`, so they reuse the whole crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI for free. To open them, an interaction sets `state.craftingStation = "villager"` (right-clicking a villager via `tryTradeAimedVillager` in `interact.ts`); the panel then shows the trades as a **Trading** book and the `craft` command runs them. Emerald is the currency — add sell offers (material → emerald) and buy offers (emerald → goods) to keep the economy two-sided. There's no persisted trade state, so this needs no save-format change.
34:## A new mob drop
39:## A new mob
46:- To make a passive animal **breedable**, add it to `FEED_ITEMS` in `lib/game/engine/systems/interact.ts` (which food puts it "in love"). Breeding itself is generic: `lib/game/engine/systems/breeding.ts` pairs two fed adults of the same kind into a baby (scaled down via `ageTimer`/`BABY_SCALE`, no drops until grown), bounded by `PASSIVE_CAP`. `MobState.fedTimer`/`ageTimer` and the tunables in `config.ts` drive it; mobs are never persisted, so this is session-only by design.
49:## A ranged weapon or ranged mob
56:## An explosion (creeper, TNT, or anything that blows up)
63:## A new player skin preset
69:## A new sound
75:## An interactive block (right-click behavior)
79:- Four reference implementations: the **door** atomically toggles matching upper/lower state IDs; the **bed** sets `state.spawnPoint` and starts the sleep fade (`state.sleepTimer`); the **furnace** opens the inventory and sets `state.craftingStation`, which unlocks its `station` recipes (see "A new item or recipe"); the **chest** opens the inventory and sets `state.openContainerIndex` (see below). A block that opens the inventory from a mouse click also needs an `openedStation`/`openedContainer` handler in `useMinecraftGame` to release pointer lock. See [architecture.md](architecture.md) for the step order.
81:## A container block (block-entity storage)
89:## A random-tick behavior (growth / spread)
94:- A growing block also needs the usual block plumbing — a `BlockId` (appended at the end of the enum so saved ids don't shift), `BLOCK_COLORS` + an atlas paint branch (plants reuse the wheat trick: a solid cube painted to read as a plant, so no new geometry), an exhaustive `GROUP_BY_BLOCK` sound entry, and `BREAK_HARDNESS`/`BLOCK_TO_SLOT`/`rollBlockDrops`. A new **material** item (e.g. bone meal) must also get an `ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS` entry, or the sprite test fails its no-magenta-checker assertion.
97:## A transient (session-only) entity (projectile, bobber, …)
103:## A worldgen structure (houses, dungeons, …)
109:## A loot table / lazy block-entity fill
114:## A status effect (timed buff / hazard)
119:- **Grant it.** A drinkable potion is a `material` item with an `effect: { id, durationSeconds }` (`ItemDef`/`InventorySlot`); the `drinkPotion` command and the `F` key apply it via `addEffect`. A brewed potion is a `station: "brewing"` recipe (see "A new item or recipe"). A hazard source just calls `addEffect` (e.g. the rotten-flesh roll in the `eatFood` case, using the injected `this.rng`).
123:## An enchantment (per-item gear modifier)
131:## A new mechanic
```

16	## A new item or recipe
17	
18	- Add to `ITEM_DEFS` in `lib/game/items.ts` — tools take `minePower`/`mineTier`/`maxDurability`, weapons `attack`/`maxDurability`, armor `armorSlot`/`defense`/`maxDurability`. `kind: "food"` items take a `hunger` value (restored on eat); `kind: "material"` items are inert craft ingredients.
19	- Give it an inventory sprite in `lib/ui/spritePixels.ts`: pickaxes, swords, and
20	  spears get one for free when named `<material>_pickaxe`, `<material>_sword`, or
21	  `<material>_spear` and the material exists in `MATERIAL_PALETTES`;
22	  food/material items need a 16×16 grid + palette in `ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS`. The
23	  sprite integrity test rejects placeholder fallbacks. A **tool with a non-material
24	  prefix** (e.g. `fishing_rod`) must either add a `MATERIAL_PALETTES` entry or use a
25	  custom `ITEM_SPRITE_GRIDS` grid AND be listed in the `customGrid` exempt set in
26	  `spritePixels.test.ts` — otherwise the tool-prefix-palette test fails.
27	- Any item with `maxDurability` is automatically non-stackable. Spears also set
28	  `meleeReach` and `throwDamage`; `systems/spears.ts` handles throwing.
29	- `ITEM_DEF_BY_ID` is derived from `ITEM_DEFS`; never edit it directly.
30	- Recipes are `{ id, label, cost: [{slotId, count}], result: {slotId, count} }` in `lib/game/recipes.ts`. An optional `station` makes a recipe station-gated: it only crafts while that station's panel is open, enforced in the `craft` command and shown locked in the recipe book. The stations are `"furnace"` (smelting), `"villager"` (a **trade** — see below), and `"brewing"` (potions, opened at a brewing stand). Adding a station means extending that union in **four** places (`Recipe.station` in `types.ts`, `craftingStation` on both `GameState` and `GameSnapshot` in `state.ts`, and the `openedStation` event), plus a `RecipeCategory` + a `recipeCategory()` branch and a `STATION_LABELS` entry (`InventoryPanel.tsx`).
31	- **Villager trades** (`lib/game/trades.ts`) ARE just `station: "villager"` recipes spread into `RECIPES`, so they reuse the whole crafting pipeline and recipe-book UI for free. To open them, an interaction sets `state.craftingStation = "villager"` (right-clicking a villager via `tryTradeAimedVillager` in `interact.ts`); the panel then shows the trades as a **Trading** book and the `craft` command runs them. Emerald is the currency — add sell offers (material → emerald) and buy offers (emerald → goods) to keep the economy two-sided. There's no persisted trade state, so this needs no save-format change.
32	- Items with durability don't stack; durability is initialized in `createSlot` and persisted in saves.
33	
34	## A new mob drop
35	
36	- Mob loot lives in `lib/game/mobLoot.ts`: add or edit the `MOB_DROPS[kind]` entries (`{ itemId, min, max, chance? }`). `rollMobDrops` is the single roll, called once from `GameEngine.removeMobAt` for every death (combat kills and daylight burns alike), so there is nothing else to wire.
37	- Every `itemId` must exist in `ITEM_DEFS`; `lib/game/mobLoot.test.ts` enforces that and the count bounds.
38	
```

=== save-format player/restore ===
9:- a block **diff** list — `changes: [blockIndex, blockId][]`; edits that revert to the generated baseline are pruned (player-placed crops, beds, furnaces, chests, and both halves/states of doors ride this list — they are ordinary block edits)
10:- inventory slots with durability **and enchantments** (36 slots; the first 9 are the hotbar). Each `SavedSlot` is `{ id, count, durability?, enchantments? }`; `enchantments` (`{ id, level }[]`) ride durable gear only and are validated on load (`restoreSlot` — known ids via `Object.hasOwn`, levels clamped to `1..ENCHANT_MAX_LEVEL`). The same path serializes chest contents
11:- `xp` (optional) — banked experience points (`XP_PER_LEVEL` points = one level). Earned from kills/ore/fishing, spent at the enchanting table. Restored by `restoreXp` (finite, ≥ 0). Unlike effects, XP is a currency and is **kept across death**
14:- player position
15:- `dayClock`, `hearts`, `hunger` (all optional) — time of day and player stats, so sleeping through the night and a hurt/hungry player survive a reload
16:- `spawnPoint` (optional, `{x,y,z}` or null) — the bed respawn point; respawn falls back to a random land point when it is null or the block there is no longer a bed
18:- `effects` (optional) — active status effects as `{ id, remaining }[]` (remaining in seconds). Drink-granted buffs (Speed/Strength/Regeneration/Fire Resistance/Water Breathing) and the rotten-flesh Poison hazard live in `state.effects` (a Map) and persist here, but are **cleared on death**, so a saved-then-reloaded world resumes whatever was active at save time. On load, `restoreEffects` (`lib/game/save.ts`) drops unknown ids and non-positive/garbage durations
19:- `lootedChests` (optional) — voxel indices of dungeon loot chests the player has already opened or broken. Dungeon chests are placed by worldgen, so their contents are **not** in `changes`/`blockEntities` until accessed; the engine fills them lazily on first access (loot seeded from `world.seed ^ index`) and records the index here. Because an emptied chest drops out of `blockEntities`, this set — not the chest's emptiness — is what prevents a re-roll on reload. The session-only set of _which_ indices are dungeon chests/spawners is not stored; it is re-derived from the seed each load via `collectDungeonSites` (`lib/world/generation.ts`). Handled by `serializeLootedChests` / `readLootedChests` in `lib/game/save.ts`
21:Each world's `SaveData` is stored in its own localStorage key, `minecraft_world_save_<worldId>` (`worldSaveKey` in `lib/game/worlds.ts`). Read/write and restore validation live in `lib/game/save.ts` — `readSave`/`writeSave` take the key as a parameter and the `Storage` is injectable for tests; `GameEngine.serialize()` produces the save from live state, and `lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts` maintains the block diff. (The legacy single-world key `SAVE_KEY` = `minecraft_save_v7` is now read only once, by the migration described under [Profiles & worlds](#profiles--worlds).)
29:(breath restores to full). The status-effect **accumulators** (`effectRegenTimer`,
35:- **v7** (XP & enchanting) — adds optional top-level `xp` and an optional `enchantments` array on each `SavedSlot`. `migrateSaveV6toV7` is a pure version bump (absent → xp 0, no enchantments). XP persists and is **kept across death** (it's a currency, not a status effect); enchantments ride the existing `inventorySlotsSnapshot`/`restoreSlot` path (shared by inventory and chests) and are validated on load. The **Enchanting Table** item saves by string id and the `EnchantingTable` `BlockId` is **appended** (id 52) so saved block ids never shift. No worldgen change — **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8**.
36:- **v6** (status effects, brewing & potions) — adds optional `effects` (`{ id, remaining }[]`). `migrateSaveV5toV6` is a pure version bump (absent → no active effects), so pre-effect saves load clean. Effects persist but are cleared on death; `serializeEffects`/`restoreEffects` (`lib/game/save.ts`) round-trip and validate them. Potions, glass bottles, and the **Brewing Stand** item save by string id; the `BrewingStand` `BlockId` is **appended at the end** of the enum (id 51) so existing saved block ids never shift, and a placed stand persists in the block diff like any block. No worldgen change — the new effects/recipes touch no generation — so **`WORLDGEN_VERSION` stays at 8** and the determinism hashes are untouched.
45:- **v4** — adds optional `blockEntities` (chest contents). `migrateSaveV3toV4` is a pure version bump (absent → restores as no containers), so **`SAVE_KEY` was unchanged** at the time (player-placed chests only; worldgen and the index formula were untouched) and v1/v2/v3 saves load fine.
46:- **v3** — adds optional `dayClock`, `hearts`, `hunger`, and `spawnPoint`. `migrateSaveV2toV3` is a pure version bump (the new fields stay absent and restore as engine defaults), so **`SAVE_KEY` is unchanged** and v1/v2 saves load fine.
55:- **`minecraft_profiles_v1`** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`) — the profile list plus `activeProfileId`. A profile is `{ id, name, skinId, createdAt }`: player identity only (name + skin). The skin moved here from the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key; a dangling `activeProfileId` is repaired on read.
67:`migrateLegacySave` (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`) runs once on first boot, gated on the **absence** of `minecraft_profiles_v1` (its presence is the already-migrated flag, which survives a rollback that re-creates the legacy key). When a legacy `minecraft_save_v7` exists it creates a default "Player" profile (carrying the old global skin) and **copies** the save into one "My World" blob, then removes the legacy key — the copy precedes the delete so a mid-migration failure can never lose the original. A brand-new player with **no** legacy save gets nothing here; the menu opens straight into the create-profile form (`ProfileSelect` renders it whenever there are zero profiles), so their first act is naming and skinning a profile.
78:- Durable gear restores at a maximum count of one per slot. The v1→v2 migration
=== testing.md coverage map ===
8:## What is covered
17:| Inventory algebra     | `lib/game/inventory.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Stack math, durable-item non-stacking, durability, crafting (including refuse-on-full), armor equip rules                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
18:| Save format           | `lib/game/save.test.ts`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Round-trips (incl. v4→v5, v5→v6, and v6→v7 migrations, `lootedChests`, the `effects` field, `xp`, and per-slot `enchantments` — round-trips + unknown-id/garbage-value dropping and level clamping), legacy-shape parsing and durable-stack splitting, corrupt-data rejection                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
19:| XP & enchanting       | `lib/game/engine/systems/xp.test.ts`, `lib/game/enchantments.test.ts`, `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts`, `components/game/{XpBar,EnchantingColumn}.test.tsx`                                                                                              | XP level/progress math, award accumulation, spend afford/refuse, per-mob/per-ore tables; XP on a kill (not a baby), the four enchant seams (Sharpness +damage, Protection +armor reduction, Efficiency ×mining speed, Unbreaking skips wear seeded), `canEnchant`/`applyEnchant` gating + immutability; the enchant command (applies + spends levels + emits; refused off-table / broke / wrong kind), right-click table opens the station; XP bar fill + hidden level 0, the EnchantingColumn afford/locked states                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

46:## Running the E2E suite
53:E2E tests assert through `window.__monecraft` (the live engine/renderer/input handle — also handy in the browser console) instead of pixels; the one render check uses `renderer.renderedTriangles()`. The shared `gamePage` fixture seeds a default profile + world and enters it through the menu (reloads auto-resume the tab's world), then fails any test that logs a console error; `menu.e2e.ts` instead drives the menus from empty storage with the plain Playwright `test`.

30	  - **Architecture**: tree building lives in `lib/game/engine/systems/treeGrowth.ts`, shared by the sapling random-tick handler and the bone-meal use; it writes through the `blockChanges` chokepoint (so the tree persists and relights) using the engine rng, and deliberately shares **no** code with worldgen's `placeTrees`, so the generation hashes stay **byte-identical**. Saplings render like wheat — a solid cube with an atlas-painted texture — so there is no new geometry path. The `Sapling` `BlockId` is appended at the end of the enum (id 50) so existing saved block ids never shift
31	
32	### Changed
33	
34	- **Hostile health visibility and durability**: zombies, skeletons, spiders, and creepers now each have **100 HP**, while the boss has **1000 HP**. Every hostile renders a procedural billboard health bar above its head that tracks damage; the existing boss HUD bar remains. This is transient simulation/render state, so there is **no save-format or worldgen impact**.
35	
36	## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-14
37	
```

  "new_string": "  - **Architecture**: tree building lives in `lib/game/engine/systems/treeGrowth.ts`, shared by the sapling random-tick handler and the bone-meal use; it writes through the `blockChanges` chokepoint (so the tree persists and relights) using the engine rng, and deliberately shares **no** code with worldgen's `placeTrees`, so the generation hashes stay **byte-identical**. Saplings render like wheat — a solid cube with an atlas-painted texture — so there is no new geometry path. The `Sapling` `BlockId` is appended at the end of the enum (id 50) so existing saved block ids never shift\n- **Recipe hints — what's missing and how to get it**: an unaffordable recipe in the inventory's recipe book is no longer a dead end. Hovering one now shows each ingredient as **have / need** (with how many more you're short), and for every missing item a **\"how to obtain it\"** hint — hunt a mob, craft/smelt/trade, mine, fish, or loot a dungeon — derived once from the existing loot/recipe tables (`lib/game/itemSources.ts`), so new recipes and drops are covered automatically. **This also repairs a latent bug**: recipe rows used the native `disabled` attribute, which suppresses hover events, so even the pre-existing \"Requires Furnace/Brewing Stand/Villager\" station hint never actually appeared; rows now use `aria-disabled` + a guarded click, staying hoverable while still unclickable. Pure UI plus a derived lookup — **no save-format or worldgen impact**.\n\n### Changed\n\n- **Hostile health visibility and durability**: zombies, skeletons, spiders, and creepers now each have **100 HP**, while the boss has **1000 HP**. Every hostile renders a procedural billboard health bar above its head that tracks damage; the existing boss HUD bar remains. This is transient simulation/render state, so there is **no save-format or worldgen impact**.\n\n### Fixed\n\n- **A corrupt save no longer loads a broken world**: the player position is validated on load (`restorePlayerPosition` in `lib/game/save.ts`) — a missing or non-finite (NaN/Infinity) coordinate is rejected, leaving the fresh spawn in place. Previously a bad value was copied straight into the player and also slipped past the `position.y < 2` auto-unstuck net (any comparison with NaN is false), so a single corrupt byte could wedge a save.\n- **The renderer no longer leaks the block-atlas texture**: `GameRenderer.dispose()` now releases the shared world/glass atlas texture (Three.js `Material.dispose()` does not dispose its map), so switching worlds no longer accumulates orphaned GPU textures.\n- **No setState or reload after unmount**: the React shell tracks and cancels its flash-message and world-reload `setTimeout`s on unmount, so leaving a world mid-timer can't fire a state update or a stray reload on a torn-down component."
}
```

All work is complete and the full verify gate is green. Here's the summary.

## What landed (branch `feat/crafting-hints-and-fixes`)

**Your finding — crafting recipe hints** (the headline)
- Hovering an unaffordable recipe now shows each ingredient as **have / need** plus a **"how to obtain it"** hint per missing item. New `lib/game/itemSources.ts` derives those hints once from the existing loot/recipe tables — priority **hunt a common mob → craft/smelt/trade → mine → fish → dungeon → boss** (so wool says "Hunt a sheep", diamond ore says "Mine Diamond Ore", not "Defeat the boss").
- Root cause fixed: rows used the native `disabled` attribute, which **suppresses hover events** — that's why nothing appeared on hover (and why the pre-existing "Requires Furnace" hint was silently broken too). Now `aria-disabled` + a guarded `onClick` keeps rows hoverable but unclickable.

**Three verified bugfixes**
- **Corrupt save → broken world**: `restorePlayerPosition` rejects missing/NaN/Infinity coords (which otherwise slipped past the `y < 2` unstuck net).
- **Texture leak**: `GameRenderer.dispose()` now releases the shared block-atlas texture.
- **Timer-after-unmount**: flash-message and reload `setTimeout`s are tracked and cancelled on unmount.

**Verify gate — all green**
- format:check ✓ · lint ✓ · typecheck ✓ · `bun test` **670 pass** ✓ · build ✓ · e2e **15 pass** ✓ (incl. the end-to-end crafting test)
- New tests: `itemSources.test.ts`, plus additions to `inventory`, `save`, `GameEngine`, and `InventoryPanel` suites (the two `.disabled` assertions were updated to `aria-disabled`).
- Docs updated: manual, reference, adding-content, save-format, testing map, and a CHANGELOG entry (Added + new Fixed section).

17 files changed, 2 new. I've **not committed** — per your usual flow I'll leave that to you. Want me to commit this as focused slices (crafting feature, then the three fixes), or as one commit?

One thing worth flagging: I noticed `docs/manual.md` says "65 recipes" while `docs/reference.md` says "66 crafting recipes" — a pre-existing inconsistency I left untouched since it's outside this change. Happy to fix it if you'd like.

> AGENT

Found it. The effect cleanup (lines 315–327) **never saves** — it only clears the interval and removes the `beforeunload` listener. Autosave is every 15s and `beforeunload` only fires on a real page reload. In dev, **Turbopack Fast Refresh remounts the component without a page reload**, so the cleanup runs, disposes the engine, and the new mount reads the stale on-disk save — losing everything since the last 15s autosave. That's your missing items.

> AGENT

Confirmed: `GameShell.tsx:81` keys `MinecraftGame` by `${world.id}:${reloadNonce}`, and `onReloadWorld` bumps the nonce → a full remount. So Load/Reset go through the same unmount path — which means a naive "save on unmount" would overwrite the disk save and **defeat Load/Reset**. I'll guard with a consume-once flag.

> AGENT

Fixed. Here's what was happening.

## Diagnosis: a save-*timing* bug, not data loss

Your items weren't corrupted — they were never saved before the remount. The game only persisted in three ways:
- a **15-second autosave timer**,
- on **`beforeunload`** (a real page reload), and
- explicit Save / Quit.

The React effect cleanup disposed the engine but **never saved**. In dev, **Turbopack Fast Refresh remounts the component without a page reload**, so `beforeunload` doesn't fire — the new mount re-reads the on-disk save, which is up to 15s stale. Anything you'd collected since the last autosave was gone. Editing code or rerunning dev mid-play triggers this every time, which is exactly what you saw.

This is **pre-existing** (not from my crafting work) — you just hit it constantly while iterating.

## The fix

`useMinecraftGame.ts` now **persists on unmount** (silently, no toast), so a Fast Refresh remount keeps your progress. It's guarded by a consume-once flag so **Load and Reset still work** — those intentionally re-read or discard the on-disk save, and `GameShell` remounts via a key bump, so a naive unmount-save would have overwritten the blob they're reloading.

## Verified — full gate green

- lint ✓ · typecheck ✓ · format:check ✓ · **670 unit tests** ✓ · build ✓ · **15 e2e** ✓ (world-switch, reload-resume, chest-persists-across-reload, save-from-pause all still pass)

Automated coverage caveat: the Fast-Refresh path itself isn't unit-testable (it needs real WebGL, which happy-dom lacks, and Playwright can't simulate Fast Refresh), so this one rests on the reasoning above plus the e2e proving the save/load lifecycle is intact. Please confirm in your dev workflow — collect a few items, save a code file to trigger a reload, and they should stick now.

Added a CHANGELOG entry under **Fixed**. Not committed yet, per your usual flow.

One note: because dev now persists across hot reloads, if you ever want a clean slate use **Reset** in the pause menu (it still wipes correctly). Want me to fold this into the commit set when you're ready?

1. `feat(crafting)` — recipe hints (Part 1)
2. `fix(save)` — validate player position on load (Part 2)
3. `fix(render)` — dispose the block-atlas texture (Part 3)
4. `fix(shell)` — persist + cancel timers on unmount (Parts 4 + 5; same file, same teardown concern)

One wrinkle I'll flag up front: `docs/testing.md` is a big aligned Markdown table, so Prettier re-aligned the *whole* table when I edited a few cells — its diff can't be cleanly split per slice. I'll put it in commit 1 (the bulk of the new test rows are crafting). `CHANGELOG.md` is prose, so I'll split it cleanly across the commits. Let me start.

31	- **Recipe hints — what's missing and how to get it**: an unaffordable recipe in the inventory's recipe book is no longer a dead end. Hovering one now shows each ingredient as **have / need** (with how many more you're short), and for every missing item a **"how to obtain it"** hint — hunt a mob, craft/smelt/trade, mine, fish, or loot a dungeon — derived once from the existing loot/recipe tables (`lib/game/itemSources.ts`), so new recipes and drops are covered automatically. **This also repairs a latent bug**: recipe rows used the native `disabled` attribute, which suppresses hover events, so even the pre-existing "Requires Furnace/Brewing Stand/Villager" station hint never actually appeared; rows now use `aria-disabled` + a guarded click, staying hoverable while still unclickable. Pure UI plus a derived lookup — **no save-format or worldgen impact**.
32	
33	### Changed
34	
35	- **Hostile health visibility and durability**: zombies, skeletons, spiders, and creepers now each have **100 HP**, while the boss has **1000 HP**. Every hostile renders a procedural billboard health bar above its head that tracks damage; the existing boss HUD bar remains. This is transient simulation/render state, so there is **no save-format or worldgen impact**.
36	
37	### Fixed
38	
39	- **Progress no longer vanishes on a dev hot reload**: the game now persists on component unmount, not only on the 15s autosave timer and `beforeunload`. Turbopack Fast Refresh remounts the game without a page reload (so `beforeunload` never fires), which previously discarded everything collected since the last autosave — most visible as **inventory items disappearing after editing code or rerunning dev**. The unmount save is silent and is deliberately **skipped for Load/Reset**, which intentionally re-read or discard the on-disk save.
40	- **A corrupt save no longer loads a broken world**: the player position is validated on load (`restorePlayerPosition` in `lib/game/save.ts`) — a missing or non-finite (NaN/Infinity) coordinate is rejected, leaving the fresh spawn in place. Previously a bad value was copied straight into the player and also slipped past the `position.y < 2` auto-unstuck net (any comparison with NaN is false), so a single corrupt byte could wedge a save.
41	- **The renderer no longer leaks the block-atlas texture**: `GameRenderer.dispose()` now releases the shared world/glass atlas texture (Three.js `Material.dispose()` does not dispose its map), so switching worlds no longer accumulates orphaned GPU textures.
42	- **No setState or reload after unmount**: the React shell tracks and cancels its flash-message and world-reload `setTimeout`s on unmount, so leaving a world mid-timer can't fire a state update or a stray reload on a torn-down component.
43	
44	## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-14
```

The unmount save is silent and is deliberately **skipped for Load/Reset**, which intentionally re-read or discard the on-disk save.\n- **A corrupt save no longer loads a broken world**: the player position is validated on load (`restorePlayerPosition` in `lib/game/save.ts`) — a missing or non-finite (NaN/Infinity) coordinate is rejected, leaving the fresh spawn in place. Previously a bad value was copied straight into the player and also slipped past the `position.y < 2` auto-unstuck net (any comparison with NaN is false), so a single corrupt byte could wedge a save.\n- **The renderer no longer leaks the block-atlas texture**: `GameRenderer.dispose()` now releases the shared world/glass atlas texture (Three.js `Material.dispose()` does not dispose its map), so switching worlds no longer accumulates orphaned GPU textures.\n- **No setState or reload after unmount**: the React shell tracks and cancels its flash-message and world-reload `setTimeout`s on unmount, so leaving a world mid-timer can't fire a state update or a stray reload on a torn-down component.\n\n## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-14",
  "new_string": "This is transient simulation/render state, so there is **no save-format or worldgen impact**.\n\n## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-14"
}

245	  return out;
246	}

248	/**
249	 * Rebuilds inventory slots from a save, dropping unknown items, clamping
250	 * counts and durability, and skipping broken gear. Supports both the current
251	 * inventorySlots shape and the legacy inventoryCounts shape. Returns null when
252	 * the save carries no inventory.
253	 */
254	export function restoreInventorySlots(save: SaveData): InventorySlot[] | null {
255	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
256	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
257	    for (let i = 0; i < Math.min(INVENTORY_SLOTS, save.inventorySlots.length); i += 1) {
258	      slots[i] = restoreSlot(save.inventorySlots[i]);
259	    }
260	    return slots;
261	  }
262	
263	  if (save.inventoryCounts) {
264	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
265	    let cursor = 0;
266	    for (const [id, raw] of Object.entries(save.inventoryCounts)) {
267	      if (!ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[id]) continue;
268	      let remaining = Math.max(0, Math.floor(raw));
269	      while (remaining > 0 && cursor < slots.length) {
270	        const add = Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(id), remaining);
271	        slots[cursor] = createSlot(id, add);
272	        cursor += 1;
273	        remaining -= add;
274	      }
275	    }
276	    return slots;
277	  }
278	
279	  return null;
280	}
281	
282	/** Restores equipped armor from a save, ignoring ids that are not valid armor for the slot. */
283	export function restoreEquippedArmor(save: SaveData): EquippedArmor | null {
284	  if (!save.equippedArmor) return null;
285	  const next = createEmptyArmorEquipment();
286	  for (const armorSlot of ARMOR_SLOTS) {
287	    const equippedId = save.equippedArmor[armorSlot];
288	    if (!equippedId) continue;
289	    const def = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[equippedId];
290	    if (def?.kind !== "armor" || def.armorSlot !== armorSlot) continue;
291	    next[armorSlot] = equippedId;
292	  }
293	  return next;
294	}
295	
296	export function restoreSelectedSlot(save: SaveData): number | null {
297	  if (typeof save.selectedSlot !== "number") return null;
298	  return normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot);
299	}
300	
301	/** Restores the day clock from a save (finite, non-negative); null if absent/invalid. */
302	export function restoreDayClock(save: SaveData): number | null {
303	  if (typeof save.dayClock !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.dayClock) || save.dayClock < 0) return null;
304	  return save.dayClock;
305	}
306	
307	/** Restores hearts clamped to 1..MAX_HEARTS; null if absent/invalid. */
308	export function restoreHearts(save: SaveData): number | null {
309	  if (typeof save.hearts !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hearts)) return null;
310	  return Math.max(1, Math.min(MAX_HEARTS, Math.floor(save.hearts)));
311	}
312	
313	/** Restores hunger clamped to 0..MAX_HUNGER; null if absent/invalid. */
314	export function restoreHungerLevel(save: SaveData): number | null {
315	  if (typeof save.hunger !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hunger)) return null;
316	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_HUNGER, Math.floor(save.hunger)));
317	}

=== restoreSlot ===
144:function restoreEnchantments(saved: SavedSlot["enchantments"]): Enchantment[] | undefined {
161:function restoreSlot(saved: SavedSlot | undefined): InventorySlot {
function restoreSlot(saved: SavedSlot | undefined): InventorySlot {
  if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0 || !ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id]) return createEmptySlot();
  const slot = createSlot(saved.id, Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(saved.id), Math.max(0, Math.floor(saved.count))));
  if ((slot.kind === "tool" || slot.kind === "weapon" || slot.kind === "armor") && slot.maxDurability) {
    if (typeof saved.durability === "number") {
      const loaded = Math.floor(saved.durability);
      if (loaded <= 0) return createEmptySlot();
      slot.durability = Math.max(1, Math.min(slot.maxDurability, loaded));
    } else {
      slot.durability = slot.maxDurability;
    }
    // Enchantments live only on durable gear; validated against the known set.
    const enchantments = restoreEnchantments(saved.enchantments);
    if (enchantments) slot.enchantments = enchantments;
  }
  return slot;
}
```

=== readSave + migration dispatch ===
export function readSave(saveKey: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): SaveData | null {
  try {
    const raw = storage.getItem(saveKey);
    if (!raw) return null;
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as SaveData | SaveDataV6 | SaveDataV5 | SaveDataV4 | SaveDataV3 | SaveDataV2 | SaveDataV1;
    if (!parsed || !Number.isFinite(parsed.seed) || !Array.isArray(parsed.changes)) return null;
    let migrated: SaveDataV2 | SaveDataV3 | SaveDataV4 | SaveDataV5 | SaveDataV6 | SaveData = parsed.version === 1 ? migrateSaveV1toV2(parsed) : parsed;
    if (migrated.version === 2) migrated = migrateSaveV2toV3(migrated);
    if (migrated.version === 3) migrated = migrateSaveV3toV4(migrated);
    if (migrated.version === 4) migrated = migrateSaveV4toV5(migrated);
    if (migrated.version === 5) migrated = migrateSaveV5toV6(migrated);
    if (migrated.version === 6) migrated = migrateSaveV6toV7(migrated);
    if (migrated.version !== 7) return null;
    return migrated;
  } catch {
    return null;
  }
}

=== legacyMigration copy logic ===
/**
 * One-time migration from the single-world era into the profile/world model.
 *
 * Before this feature the game kept one world under `minecraft_save_v7` and a
 * global skin under `minecraft_skin_v1`. On first boot after the feature lands,
 * we fold those into a default "Player" profile (carrying the old skin) plus a
 * single "My World" pointing at a copy of the old save blob.
 *
 * Idempotent and gated on the *profiles manifest's absence* — its presence is
 * the "already migrated" flag, so this survives a rollback that re-creates the
 * legacy key. Storage and the createdAt/id sources are injectable for tests.
 */

import { SAVE_KEY } from "./config";
import { resolveDeps, type ManifestDeps } from "./manifest";
import { createProfile, PROFILES_KEY } from "./profiles";
import { readSave, writeSave } from "./save";
import { readSkinSettings } from "./skinSettings";
import { createWorld, worldSaveKey } from "./worlds";

export const LEGACY_WORLD_NAME = "My World";

export function migrateLegacySave(deps: ManifestDeps = {}): void {
  const { storage } = resolveDeps(deps);

  // Presence of the profiles manifest means the feature has already run here.
  if (storage.getItem(PROFILES_KEY) !== null) return;

  const legacy = readSave(SAVE_KEY, storage); // runs the v1->v5 migration chain

  // Brand-new player (no legacy save): create nothing. The menu greets them with
  // the create-profile form so their first act is naming + skinning a profile.
  if (!legacy) return;

  const { skinId } = readSkinSettings(storage); // old global skin seeds the default profile
  const profile = createProfile("Player", skinId, deps); // writes the manifest, becomes active
  const world = createWorld(profile.id, LEGACY_WORLD_NAME, String(legacy.seed), deps);
  try {
    // Copy (not move) so a mid-migration failure can never lose the original.
    writeSave(worldSaveKey(world.id), legacy, storage);
    try {
      storage.removeItem(SAVE_KEY);
    } catch {
      // Leaving the legacy key just wastes a little space; the copy is authoritative.
    }
  } catch {
    // Quota failure copying the blob (storage briefly holds both): the world
    // record still exists and will boot fresh from its stored seed; the legacy
    // key is left intact rather than risk losing the original edits.
  }
}
```

I've now traced the entire load path and I want to be straight with you about what the code can and cannot do:

- **`restoreSlot`** only drops a slot if its item id is unknown, or it's gear at 0 durability. The only item id ever removed in recent history is the old `"food"` placeholder — not "many".
- **The migration chain** (v2→v7) are pure version bumps that carry `inventorySlots` forward untouched; only ancient **v1** saves get re-packed (40→36 slots, dropping overflow beyond slot 36).
- **Slot counts** (9 hotbar / 36 inventory) and **stack size** (99) haven't changed recently.
- The **profiles/worlds migration** copies the full save blob, preserving inventory.
- **My branch touches none of this** — no item ids, no restore/migrate code. And the save-on-unmount I added can only write the engine's *current* inventory; it can't turn a readable save into a starter loadout.

So a *permanent load-time drop of normal items* isn't supported by the code I can see. That means the cause is something my static read can't determine — I need a few specifics from you to pin it down (the answers point at very different fixes).

This is the key data. **Partial loss ("real items minus many"), on a manual Cmd+R, same world** — that's classic "lost progress since the last durable save," not a load-time drop. **That's the smoking gun.** Look at lines 165–168: the reload-persistence e2e test **manually** does `localStorage.setItem(..., engine.serialize())` *before* `page.reload()` — it deliberately bypasses `beforeunload`. So the test suite has never actually verified that a plain reload persists; the author worked around it. That's strong evidence `beforeunload` doesn't reliably save here, which is exactly your symptom: on Cmd+R you lose everything since the last 15s autosave → "real items, minus many."

> AGENT

The pause-menu test (line 240) clicks **"Save Game"** before reloading — another explicit save. So **neither** reload test exercises `beforeunload`; both work around it. That confirms the diagnosis: normal play relies only on the 15s autosave, and a plain Cmd+R doesn't reliably persist → you lose recent items.

The fix is the modern, reliable save-on-leave pattern: `pagehide` + `visibilitychange`(hidden), which fire dependably on reload/close/background (MDN explicitly recommends these over `beforeunload`). Let me do this properly with a reproducing e2e test first. Let me check the test helpers:

  private respawn(): void {
    const state = this.state;
    const bed = state.spawnPoint;
    if (bed && state.world.get(bed.x, bed.y, bed.z) === BlockId.Bed) {
      // Respawn standing on the bed; the bed block is non-solid head room above it.
      state.player.position.set(bed.x + 0.5, bed.y + 1.05, bed.z + 0.5);
    } else {
      const spawn = randomLandPointNear(state.world, this.surfaceYAt, state.world.sizeX / 2, state.world.sizeZ / 2, RENDER_RADIUS * 0.9, this.rng);
      state.player.position.set(spawn.x, spawn.y + 2, spawn.z);
    }
    state.player.velocity.set(0, 0, 0);
    state.player.pitch = 0;
    clearEffects(state);
    resetMining(state);
    state.thrownSpears = [];
    state.fishing = null;
    state.worldMeshDirty = true;
    this.events.push({ type: "respawned" });
  }

> AGENT

Good — that rules out another theory: **death/respawn keeps your inventory** (no clearing), and the **plain-reload test passes**, so inventory does survive a normal reload on this branch. Combined with everything else, I genuinely can't reproduce a code path that drops "many" items, and my branch doesn't touch inventory restore at all.

So I've hit the limit of what static analysis + a synthetic repro can tell me — I need to see *your actual save* to find what's being dropped. Here's a precise way to capture it.

**Right before you reload (while you can still see all your items), open DevTools Console (Cmd+Opt+J) and paste this:**

```js
(() => {
  const wid = JSON.parse(sessionStorage.getItem('monecraft_active_session')).worldId;
  const saved = JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('minecraft_world_save_' + wid) || '{}');
  const live = window.__monecraft.engine.state.inventory.filter(s => s.id).map(s => s.id + '×' + s.count);
  const onDisk = (saved.inventorySlots || []).filter(s => s && s.id).map(s => s.id + '×' + s.count);
  console.log('save version:', saved.version);
  console.log('LIVE   :', live.join(', '));
  console.log('ON DISK:', onDisk.join(', '));
})();
```

**Then reload (Cmd+R), and run the exact same snippet again.** Paste both outputs (before and after).

That pins it down precisely:
- If **LIVE has items that ON DISK is missing** *before* reload → the save isn't capturing them (a save-timing issue, despite the reload-test passing — your real workflow differs somehow).
- If **ON DISK has them before but they're gone after** → something overwrites the save on load.
- If **ON DISK has them but LIVE is missing after reload** → the restore is dropping them (and we'll see exactly which item ids).

Two quick questions while you grab that:
1. Does it happen on **every** plain Cmd+R, or only the **first** reload right after switching to this branch / a new dev build?
2. Are the vanished items a particular kind (tools/armor? the most *recently* collected? random)?

I added a reload-persistence e2e test in the process (collect → plain reload → assert) — it was a real gap in coverage, and it passes — but I'm holding off on any further code change until your console output shows me what's actually being dropped. I don't want to ship another fix that misses the real cause.

<details>
<summary>📝 Walkthrough</summary>

## Walkthrough

This PR adds ingredient have/need hover tooltips with "how to obtain" hints to the recipe book, switching recipe buttons from native `disabled` to `aria-disabled` with a guarded click. It also adds corrupt-save player-position validation (`restorePlayerPosition`), fixes a GPU texture leak in `GameRenderer`, and fixes React timer and unmount-save lifecycle bugs in `useMinecraftGame`.

## Changes

**Recipe tooltip hover feature**

| Layer / File(s) | Summary |
|---|---|
| **`itemSources` module: source-hint precomputation** <br> `lib/game/itemSources.ts`, `lib/game/itemSources.test.ts` | New module builds a precomputed `SOURCE_HINTS` map from mob/crafting/mining/fishing/dungeon/boss tables via a priority-ordered IIFE; exports `itemSourceHint(itemId)`; covered by a Bun test suite asserting hunt, craft, mine, fish, boss, and unknown-item cases. |
| **`ingredientStatus` helper: have/need counts** <br> `lib/game/inventory.ts`, `lib/game/inventory.test.ts` | Adds `IngredientStatus` type and `ingredientStatus(slots, recipe)` function computing per-ingredient have/need from `countsById`; new test asserts correct values including absent-ingredient case. |
| **`InventoryPanel`: `aria-disabled`, guarded click, and `recipeTooltip`** <br> `components/game/InventoryPanel.tsx`, `app/ui.css`, `components/game/InventoryPanel.test.tsx` | Introduces `recipeTooltip` building `TooltipContent` for locked/craftable/unaffordable states; recipe buttons switch from native `disabled` to `is-disabled` CSS class + `aria-disabled` + guarded `onClick`; CSS hover/disabled rules updated from `:disabled` pseudo-class to `.is-disabled`; tests verify ARIA semantics, click guard, and tooltip content. |
| **Recipe tooltip docs and changelog** <br> `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/manual.md`, `docs/reference.md`, `docs/adding-content.md` | CHANGELOG documents the hover improvement and `aria-disabled` fix; manual and reference docs explain dimmed-recipe hover behavior; adding-content doc notes `MINE_OVERRIDES` usage. |
